# List 27

## Unit 1

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>veracious</b></del></td><td>/vəˈreɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>真实准确的；诚实的</td><td>a veracious witness<br>诚实的证人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court relied on one veracious witness to settle the case.  法庭依靠一位诚实的证人来了结此案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 honest</td><td>/ˈɑːnəst/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>诚实的，正直的</td><td>an honest answer<br>一个诚实的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Give me an honest answer, even if it disappoints me.  给我一个诚实的答复，即使会让我失望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 truthful</td><td>/ˈtruːθfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>诚实的，如实的</td><td>a truthful answer<br>一个诚实的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Give me a truthful answer about where you were.  告诉我你当时在哪里，要说实话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dishonest</td><td>/dɪˈsɑːnəst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不诚实的，欺骗的</td><td>a dishonest business practice<br>不诚实的商业做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company used dishonest advertising to attract careless customers.  公司用不诚实的广告吸引不细心的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lying</td><td>/ˈlaɪɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>撒谎的；说谎</td><td>a lying witness<br>撒谎的证人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He called the report lying propaganda and walked out.  他称那份报告是撒谎的宣传，然后走了出去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mendacious</td><td>/menˈdeɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>撒谎的，虚假的</td><td>a mendacious account<br>虚假的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His mendacious account of the accident collapsed under a few simple questions.  他对事故的虚假陈述在几个简单问题下就站不住脚了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 prevaricating</del></td><td>/prəˈverəkeɪtɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>支吾其词的，说谎的</td><td>a prevaricating witness<br>支吾其词的证人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prevaricating official finally admitted the file existed.  那位支吾其词的官员终于承认文件存在。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 untruthful</td><td>/ʌnˈtruːθfəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不诚实的，虚假的</td><td>an untruthful answer<br>不实的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her untruthful answers soon caught the officer's attention.  她不实的回答很快引起了警官的注意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>verbatim</b></td><td>/vɜːrˈbeɪtɪm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>副词、形容词</td><td>逐字地，一字不差地</td><td>quote him verbatim<br>一字不差地引用他</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The clerk copied the confession verbatim into the official record.  书记员把供词一字不差地抄进了正式记录。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ad verbum</td><td>/æd ˈvɜrbəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>副词</td><td>逐字地，照字面</td><td>translate ad verbum<br>逐字翻译</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The clerk copied the oath ad verbum into the register.  书记员把誓词逐字抄进登记簿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 directly</td><td>/dəˈrektli/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>副词</td><td>直接地，如实地</td><td>quote directly<br>直接引用</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She answered directly instead of repeating the official line.  她如实作答，而不是重复官方说法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exactly</td><td>/ɪɡˈzæktli/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>副词</td><td>确切地，精确地</td><td>exactly as written<br>与所写完全一致</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Repeat exactly what the caller said, word for word.  把来电者的话一字不差地重复一遍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 word for word</td><td>/ˈwɜrd fər ˈwɜrd/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>副词</td><td>逐字地，一字不差</td><td>repeat it word for word<br>一字不差地重复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The child recited the poem word for word without a pause.  孩子一字不差地背完了那首诗，没有停顿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inaccurately</td><td>/ɪnˈækjərətli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>副词</td><td>不准确地</td><td>inaccurately reported<br>报道不准确</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The figures were inaccurately copied from the original survey.  这些数字是从原始调查中不准确地抄来的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 inexactly</del></td><td>/ˌɪnɪɡˈzæktli/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>副词</td><td>不确切地，含糊地</td><td>inexactly translated<br>翻译得不确切</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The term was inexactly rendered in the English edition.  这个术语在英文版中翻译得不够确切。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>verbose</b></td><td>/vɜːrˈboʊs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冗长的，啰嗦的</td><td>a verbose explanation<br>冗长的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her verbose report could have said the same thing in two pages.  她冗长的报告本可以用两页说完同样的内容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 circuitous</td><td>/sərˈkjuːɪtəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迂回的，不直接的</td><td>a circuitous route<br>迂回的路线</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We took a circuitous route to avoid the flooded highway.  我们绕了一条迂回的路以避开被淹的公路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 circumlocutory</del></td><td>/ˌsɜrkəmˈlɑkjətɔri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迂回冗赘的，绕弯子的</td><td>circumlocutory replies<br>绕弯子的答复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His circumlocutory answers hid a very simple refusal.  他绕弯子的答复掩盖了一个简单的拒绝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 diffuse</td><td>/dɪfˈjuːs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冗长散漫的；扩散的</td><td>a diffuse and wordy essay<br>散漫冗长的文章</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His argument was far too diffuse to convince a careful reader.  他的论证过于散漫，无法说服细心的读者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 garrulous</td><td>/ˈɡærələs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>啰嗦的，话多令人厌烦的</td><td>a garrulous neighbor<br>唠叨的邻居</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our garrulous neighbor talked for an hour about his lawn.  我们唠叨的邻居就他的草坪讲了一个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 long-winded</td><td>/ˈlɔːŋwɪndɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冗长啰嗦的</td><td>a long-winded introduction<br>冗长的开场白</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The chairman's long-winded report emptied half the meeting room.  主席冗长的报告让会场走了一半人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prolix</td><td>/ˈproʊlɪks/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>啰嗦的，冗长的</td><td>prolix legal prose<br>冗长的法律文字</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge criticized the prolix brief for burying its argument.  法官批评这份冗长的书状埋没了自己的论点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rambling</td><td>/ˈræmblɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>漫无目的的，散漫的</td><td>a rambling explanation<br>散漫的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave a rambling answer that satisfied nobody present.  他给出散漫的回答，在场无人满意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 windy</td><td>/ˈwɪndi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冗长的</td><td>a windy speech<br>冗长空洞的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His windy speech said very little in more than an hour.  他冗长的演讲讲了一个多小时却几乎没什么内容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 brief</td><td>/ˈbriːf/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>简短的，简洁的</td><td>a brief summary<br>简短的总结</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave a brief account of the accident to the police.  他向警方简短叙述了事故经过。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 compact</td><td>/ˈkɑːmpækt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>契约，协议；紧凑的</td><td>sign a compact<br>签订协议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two nations signed a compact to protect the shared river.  两国签订协议保护共有的河流。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 concise</td><td>/kənˈsaɪs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>简明的，简洁的</td><td>a concise explanation<br>简明的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Keep your answer concise; the interviewer has only ten minutes.  回答要简洁，面试官只有十分钟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pithy</td><td>/ˈpɪθi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>简练的，言简意赅的</td><td>a pithy remark<br>精练的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her pithy summary captured the whole debate in two sentences.  她简练的总结用两句话概括了整场辩论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 succinct</td><td>/səkˈsɪŋkt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>简明的，简洁的</td><td>a succinct summary<br>简洁的总结</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her succinct summary covered the entire report in three short paragraphs.  她简洁的总结用三小段概括了整份报告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 terse</td><td>/tɜːrs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>简洁的，简明的</td><td>a terse reply<br>简洁的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His terse note said only that the deal had collapsed.  他简短的字条只说交易破裂了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>verdant</b></td><td>/ˈvɜːrdnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（因长满植物而）翠绿的，郁郁葱葱的</td><td>verdant hills<br>翠绿的山丘</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After the rains the verdant valley filled with wildflowers.  雨后，翠绿的山谷开满了野花。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 green</td><td>/ɡriːn/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无经验的</td><td>green recruits<br>毫无经验的新兵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The green interns needed close supervision during their very first month.  这些毫无经验的实习生在最初一个月需要密切的指导。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grown</td><td>/ˈɡroʊn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>长成的，繁茂的</td><td>fully grown trees<br>长成的大树</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Fully grown trees now shade the whole southern side of the house.  长成的大树如今为房子南侧全部遮上了阴凉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 leafy</td><td>/ˈliːfi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>多叶的，绿叶茂密的</td><td>a leafy suburb<br>绿树成荫的郊区</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They walked along a leafy lane toward the old church.  他们沿着绿荫小路走向古教堂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 luxuriant</td><td>/lʌɡˈʒʊriənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>茂盛的，繁茂的</td><td>luxuriant vegetation<br>茂盛的植被</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Luxuriant ferns covered the damp floor of the forest.  茂盛的蕨类覆盖了潮湿的林地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overgrown</td><td>/ˌoʊvərˈɡroʊn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>杂草丛生的，蔓生的</td><td>an overgrown garden<br>杂草丛生的花园</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The path was overgrown after two summers of neglect.  疏于打理两个夏天后，小路已杂草丛生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 barren</td><td>/ˈbærən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫瘠的；无成果的</td><td>barren land<br>贫瘠的土地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing grows on the barren land beyond the last village.  最后一个村子之外的贫瘠土地上什么都长不出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 impoverished</td><td>/ɪmˈpɑːvərɪʃt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫困的；贫瘠的</td><td>an impoverished region<br>贫困地区</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 War left the once-prosperous city utterly impoverished for many years.  战争让这座曾繁荣的城市多年极度贫困。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 infertile</td><td>/ɪnˈfɜːrtəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫瘠的；不育的</td><td>infertile ground<br>贫瘠的土地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of drought left the ground almost completely infertile.  多年干旱使土地几乎完全贫瘠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 leafless</td><td>/ˈliːfləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无叶的，光秃的</td><td>leafless branches<br>光秃的枝条</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Leafless poplars lined the road all through February.  整个二月路边都是光秃的白杨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sterile</td><td>/ˈsterəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫瘠的；无菌的</td><td>sterile medical instruments<br>无菌医疗器械</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Surgeons must use sterile instruments to prevent infection during any operation.  外科医生必须使用无菌器械，以防手术中感染。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>verify</b></td><td>/ˈverɪfaɪ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>证实，核实</td><td>verify the data<br>核实这些数据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two independent labs will verify the results before publication.  两家独立实验室将在发表前核实这些结果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 attest</td><td>/əˈtest/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>证实，为…作证</td><td>attest to his good character<br>证明他品行良好</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Several neighbors will attest to his good character in court.  几位邻居将在法庭上证明他品行良好。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 authenticate</td><td>/ɔˈθentəˌkeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>证实，鉴定为真</td><td>authenticate the painting<br>鉴定画作真伪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Experts were called in to authenticate the painting's signature.  专家被请来鉴定这幅画签名的真伪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 certify</td><td>/ˈsɜːrtəˌfaɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>证明，认证</td><td>certify the results as valid<br>证明结果有效</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An independent lab must certify that the water is safe.  必须由独立实验室证明水质安全。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 corroborate</td><td>/kəˈrɑːbəreɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>用证据或权威证实；为…提供证据，支持</td><td>corroborate the witness's story<br>证实证人的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two neighbors corroborated the driver's account of the accident that night.  两位邻居证实了司机对那晚事故的说法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 substantiate</td><td>/səbˈstænʃieɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>证实</td><td>substantiate the claim<br>证实这一说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No evidence has emerged to substantiate his serious accusations.  没有证据能证实他严重的指控。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 support</td><td>/səˈpɔːrt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>支持，支撑</td><td>support a proposal<br>支持一项提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most members voted to support the new proposal.  大多数成员投票支持这项新提案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 validate</td><td>/ˈvælədeɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>证实，确认；使有效</td><td>validate the results<br>验证这些结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Independent labs validated the results within three months.  独立实验室在三个月内验证了这些结果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vindicate</td><td>/ˈvɪndɪkeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>为…平反，为…辩护，使无罪；证明，证实</td><td>vindicate one's reputation<br>为自己的名誉洗冤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The results vindicated her unusual approach.  结果证明了她那不寻常的方法是对的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 authentic</td><td>/ɔːˈθentɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>真实的，非仿造的</td><td>authentic Italian cuisine<br>正宗的意大利菜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The museum confirmed the letter was an authentic original.  博物馆确认这封信是真实的原件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 credible</td><td>/ˈkredəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可信的，可靠的</td><td>a credible witness<br>可信的证人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prosecution produced a credible witness at the last moment.  检方在最后一刻找出了一名可信的证人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 genuine</td><td>/ˈdʒenjəwən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>真正的，真实的；真诚的</td><td>genuine leather<br>真皮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Experts confirmed that the signature on the letter was genuine.  专家确认信上的签名是真的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 real</td><td>/ˈriːl/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>真实的，真正的</td><td>a real event<br>真实事件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film is based on a real event from 1943.  这部电影基于1943年的一件真实事件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 true</td><td>/ˈtruː/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>真实的，真正的</td><td>a true story<br>一个真实的故事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film is based on a true story from wartime.  这部电影取材于战时的一个真实故事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unquestionable</td><td>/ənˈkwestʃənəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>毫无疑问的</td><td>unquestionable integrity<br>毋庸置疑的正直</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His loyalty to the team is unquestionable.  他对团队的忠诚毫无疑问。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bona fide</td><td>/ˌboʊnə ˈfaɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>真诚的，真实的，善意的</td><td>a bona fide offer<br>真诚的报价</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only bona fide students may borrow books from this library.  只有真正的在校学生才能从本馆借书。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disprove</td><td>/ˌdɪsˈpruːv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>反驳，证明为假</td><td>disprove the theory<br>驳倒这一理论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Magellan's voyage finally disproved the belief in a flat earth.  麦哲伦的航行最终驳倒了地球是平的这一信念。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rebut</td><td>/rɪˈbʌt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>反驳，驳斥</td><td>rebut the accusation<br>反驳这项指控</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She rebutted every charge with documents and dated photographs.  她用文件和标注日期的照片反驳了每项指控。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 refute</td><td>/rɪˈfjuːt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>驳倒，反驳</td><td>refute the claim<br>驳斥这一说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scientist used new data to refute the claim about rising temperatures.  这位科学家用新数据反驳了关于气温上升的说法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bogus</td><td>/ˈboʊɡəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>伪造的，假冒的</td><td>a bogus claim<br>虚假的索赔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police discovered a stack of bogus passports in the van.  警方在货车里发现一叠伪造护照。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 counterfeit</td><td>/ˈkaʊntərfɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>仿制的，假冒的；仿制</td><td>counterfeit banknotes<br>伪造的钞票</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police seized thousands of counterfeit banknotes hidden in the delivery van.  警方缴获了藏在送货车里的数千张假钞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fake</td><td>/ˈfeɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词、动词</td><td>假的，伪造的；伪造品</td><td>a fake diploma<br>假文凭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The gallery unknowingly displayed a fake for almost ten years.  这家画廊不知情地展出了一件赝品近十年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 false</td><td>/ˈfɔːls/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>虚假的，不真诚的</td><td>a false smile<br>虚假的笑容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His false praise fooled nobody at the small dinner table.  他虚假的赞美在那张小餐桌上骗不了任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mock</td><td>/ˈmɑːk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>假的，模拟的；嘲笑</td><td>a mock trial<br>模拟审判</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students held a mock election to learn how voting works.  学生举行模拟选举以了解投票如何运作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 phony</td><td>/ˈfoʊni/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>假的，冒充的；骗子</td><td>a phony accent<br>假装的口音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He used a phony name when renting the small apartment.  他租那间小公寓时用了假名。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pseudo</td><td>/ˈsuːdoʊ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>假的，冒充的</td><td>pseudo science<br>伪科学</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The book dresses up old superstitions as pseudo science.  这本书把旧迷信包装成伪科学。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sham</td><td>/ʃæm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>掩饰，假装；虚假的</td><td>a sham marriage<br>假婚姻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The couple admitted their marriage was a sham arranged for the visa.  这对夫妻承认他们的婚姻是为签证安排的假婚姻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 spurious</td><td>/ˈspjʊriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>假的，伪造的</td><td>spurious claims<br>虚假的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The website spread spurious claims about the new vaccine.  该网站传播关于新疫苗的虚假说法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>verisimilar</b></del></td><td>/ˌverɪˈsɪmɪlər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>似乎真实的</td><td>a verisimilar account<br>似乎真实的叙述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel gives a verisimilar account of daily life in wartime.  这部小说对战时日常生活作了似乎真实的描述。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 likely</td><td>/ˈlaɪkli/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>可能的；看来可信的</td><td>the likely explanation<br>看来可信的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The most likely explanation is simple human error.  最可能的解释就是简单的人为失误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 plausible</td><td>/ˈplɔːzəbl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可信的</td><td>a plausible explanation<br>一个可信的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave a plausible explanation, though nobody could actually verify it.  他给出了一个可信的解释，尽管无人能真正核实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 probable</td><td>/ˈprɑːbəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>很可能的，有可能的</td><td>the probable outcome<br>很可能的结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A narrow victory seems the probable outcome of the election.  小幅胜出似乎是这次选举很可能的结果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 implausible</td><td>/ˌɪmˈplɔːzəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以置信的，不像真的</td><td>an implausible excuse<br>难以置信的借口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His account of the missing keys sounded wholly implausible.  他对钥匙丢失的说法听来全然不可信。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 incredible</td><td>/ˌɪnˈkredəbəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以置信的</td><td>an incredible excuse<br>难以置信的借口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She offered an incredible excuse that nobody in the room believed.  她给出一个难以置信的借口，屋里没人相信。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unbelievable</td><td>/ˌʌnbəˈliːvəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无法相信的</td><td>an unbelievable claim<br>无法相信的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His unbelievable claim about the treasure amused the whole village.  他关于宝藏的荒唐说法逗乐了整个村子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>vile</b></td><td>/vaɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>丑陋的；（道德上）可鄙的，卑鄙的</td><td>a vile insult<br>卑劣的侮辱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He shouted a vile insult and was quickly escorted outside.  他喊出一句卑劣的侮辱，很快被带了出去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grotesque</td><td>/ɡroʊˈtesk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难看的</td><td>a grotesque figure<br>难看的形象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mask was so grotesque that the youngest child began to cry.  那副面具太难看了，最小的孩子哭了起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hideous</td><td>/ˈhɪdiəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非常丑陋的</td><td>a hideous mask<br>丑陋可怕的面具</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They tore down the hideous concrete tower that blocked the view.  他们拆掉了那座挡住视野的丑陋混凝土塔楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 homely</td><td>/ˈhoʊmli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>其貌不扬的，朴素简单的</td><td>a homely face<br>其貌不扬的脸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had a homely but kind face that put strangers at ease.  她有一张其貌不扬却和善的脸，让陌生人感到自在。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 monstrous</td><td>/ˈmɑːnstrəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的；骇人的</td><td>a monstrous wave<br>巨浪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A monstrous wave rose above the tiny fishing boat.  一个巨浪在小渔船上方涌起。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 base</td><td>/ˈbeɪs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑劣的，低下的</td><td>base motives<br>卑劣的动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He acted from base motives of greed and envy.  他出于贪婪和嫉妒的卑劣动机行事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 contemptible</td><td>/kənˈtemptəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可鄙的，卑劣的</td><td>contemptible behavior<br>卑劣的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cheating a blind beggar is a contemptible thing to do.  欺骗盲丐是可鄙的行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 despicable</td><td>/dɪˈspɪkəbl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人鄙视的</td><td>a despicable act<br>令人鄙视的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Stealing from a charity is a despicable way to make money.  从慈善机构偷钱是一种令人鄙视的赚钱方式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 detestable</td><td>/dɪˈtɛstəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可憎的，令人厌恶的</td><td>a detestable habit<br>令人厌恶的习惯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He found the general's casual cruelty absolutely detestable.  他觉得那位将军随意的残暴极其可憎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dishonorable</td><td>/dɪˈsɑːnərəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不光彩的，可耻的</td><td>a dishonorable discharge<br>不光彩的除役</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Betraying an old friend for money is plainly dishonorable.  为钱背叛老友显然可耻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 execrable</td><td>/ˈeksɪkrəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>糟糕的，恶劣的</td><td>execrable table manners<br>极糟糕的餐桌礼仪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hotel served execrable coffee at breakfast every morning.  酒店每天早餐提供的咖啡都糟糕透了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ignominious</td><td>/ˌɪɡnəˈmɪniəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可耻的，不光彩的</td><td>an ignominious defeat<br>可耻的失败</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The season ended in ignominious defeat at home.  这个赛季以主场可耻的失利告终。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mean</td><td>/miːn/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑贱的；吝啬的</td><td>a mean little room<br>简陋卑贱的小屋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The family lived in a mean little room above a noisy printing shop.  一家人住在嘈杂印刷店楼上一间简陋的小屋里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nasty</td><td>/ˈnæsti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的，肮脏的；恶劣的</td><td>a nasty smell<br>难闻的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A nasty stain spread slowly across the pale carpet.  一块难看的污渍在浅色地毯上慢慢蔓延。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 paltry</td><td>/ˈpɔːltri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无价值的；微不足道的</td><td>a paltry sum<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They offered a paltry sum for a painting worth thousands of dollars.  他们为一幅价值数千美元的画只出了微不足道的一笔钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sordid</td><td>/ˈsɔːrdɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>肮脏的，不干净的；卑鄙的</td><td>a sordid little room<br>肮脏的小房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They lived for years in a sordid little room without heat.  他们在没有暖气的肮脏小屋里住了多年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wretched</td><td>/ˈretʃɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极差的；沮丧的</td><td>wretched living conditions<br>恶劣的居住条件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Families lived in wretched conditions beside the flooded riverbank.  一些家庭在泛滥的河岸边过着极差的生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 aesthetic</td><td>/esˈθetɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>审美的，美的</td><td>aesthetic appeal<br>审美上的吸引力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The building's aesthetic appeal outweighs its many practical problems.  这栋楼的美感胜过它的诸多实用缺陷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 attractive</td><td>/əˈtræktɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有吸引力的，迷人的</td><td>an attractive offer<br>有吸引力的提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company made him an attractive offer he could not refuse.  公司给了他一个无法拒绝的诱人条件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 beautiful</td><td>/ˈbjuːtəfəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>美丽的，美好的</td><td>a beautiful view<br>美丽的景色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 From the hill we had a beautiful view of the whole bay.  从山上我们看到整个海湾的美丽景色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 comely</td><td>/ˈkʌmli/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>秀丽的，好看的</td><td>a comely young woman<br>秀丽的年轻女子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A comely young woman greeted us politely at the gate.  一位秀丽的年轻女子在门口礼貌地迎接我们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cute</td><td>/ˈkjuːt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可爱的，讨人喜欢的</td><td>a cute puppy<br>可爱的小狗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children thought the muddy little dog was cute.  孩子们觉得那只满身泥的小狗很可爱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fair</td><td>/ˈfer/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>公正的；相当的；晴朗的</td><td>a fair trial<br>公正的审判</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Everyone deserves a fair hearing before any decision is made.  在做出任何决定之前，人人都应得到公正的申辩机会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gorgeous</td><td>/ˈɡɔːrdʒəs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极美的，绚丽的</td><td>a gorgeous sunset<br>绚丽的落日</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We watched a gorgeous sunset from the hotel balcony.  我们在酒店阳台上看了一场绚丽的落日。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 handsome</td><td>/ˈhænsəm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>英俊的，好看的</td><td>a handsome young man<br>英俊的年轻人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was a handsome man with unusually kind eyes.  他是个英俊的男人，眼神格外和善。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ravishing</td><td>/ˈrævɪʃɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极美的，令人陶醉的</td><td>a ravishing beauty<br>绝色的美人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She looked absolutely ravishing in her long silver evening gown.  她穿着银色晚礼长裙，美得令人陶醉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 seemly</td><td>/ˈsiːmli/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>得体的，合宜的</td><td>seemly conduct in public<br>公共场合得体的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher expects seemly conduct from her students in public places.  老师期望学生在公共场所举止得体。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 honorable</td><td>/ˈɑːnərəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>光荣的，可敬的</td><td>an honorable retreat<br>光荣的撤退</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She made the honorable choice and returned all the money.  她做出可敬的选择，把钱全退了回去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lofty</td><td>/ˈlɔːfti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>崇高的；自大的</td><td>a lofty tone<br>自大的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She dismissed our idea with a lofty wave of her hand.  她自大地一挥手就否定了我们的想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 noble</td><td>/ˈnoʊbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>高贵的，贵族的</td><td>a noble bearing<br>高贵的气派</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The horse moved with a noble grace across the field.  那匹马以高贵的优雅姿态穿过田野。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 straight</td><td>/ˈstreɪt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>直的，笔直的</td><td>a straight road<br>笔直的道路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Drive straight ahead until you reach the second traffic light.  一直往前开，直到第二个红绿灯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 upright</td><td>/ˈʌpraɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>正直的，诚实的；竖直的</td><td>an upright citizen<br>一位正直的公民</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors described him as an upright and reliable man.  邻居们说他是个正直可靠的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 venerable</td><td>/ˈvenərəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>古老而受尊敬的</td><td>a venerable institution<br>历史悠久的机构</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The venerable library has served scholars for over four centuries.  这座古老而受人尊敬的图书馆已为学者服务了四百多年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 virtuous</td><td>/ˈvɜːrtʃuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>品德高尚的，正直的</td><td>a virtuous life<br>高尚的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He led a quiet, virtuous life and helped his neighbors freely.  他过着平静高尚的生活，乐于帮助邻里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>vernacular</b></td><td>/vərˈnækjələr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>方言；非正式的，口头的</td><td>the local vernacular<br>当地方言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wrote his plays in the vernacular rather than in formal Latin.  他用方言而非正式的拉丁语写作剧本。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 argot</td><td>/ˈɑːrɡət/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>行话，隐语</td><td>thieves' argot<br>盗贼的隐语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel is thick with the argot of dockworkers.  这部小说充满码头工人的行话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cant</td><td>/ˈkænt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>行话；伪善之言</td><td>political cant<br>政治套话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His speech was pure cant dressed up as principle.  他的演讲不过是伪装成原则的套话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dialect</td><td>/ˈdaɪəˌlekt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>方言，土语</td><td>a local dialect<br>当地方言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her grandmother still speaks a dialect few young people understand.  她祖母仍说着一种少有年轻人懂的方言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jargon</td><td>/ˈdʒɑːrɡən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>行业术语</td><td>technical jargon<br>专业术语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report was buried in technical jargon that ordinary readers could not follow.  这份报告埋没在普通读者无法理解的专业术语里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lingo</td><td>/ˈlɪŋɡoʊ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>行话，隐语；外语</td><td>computer lingo<br>电脑行话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I could not follow the lawyers' lingo at all.  我完全跟不上律师们的行话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slang</td><td>/ˈslæŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>俚语，俗语</td><td>street slang<br>街头俚语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel is full of street slang that dates it precisely.  这部小说满是街头俚语，正好标出了它的年代。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conversational</td><td>/ˌkɑːnvərˈseɪʃənəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>健谈的，好交谈的</td><td>a conversational host<br>健谈的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our conversational host kept every guest talking until well past midnight.  我们健谈的主人让每位客人一直聊到午夜之后。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 informal</td><td>/ɪnˈfɔːrməl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非正式的，随意的</td><td>an informal meeting<br>非正式会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They settled the dispute through informal talks over coffee.  他们在咖啡桌旁通过非正式交谈解决了争端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 nonliterary</del></td><td>/nɑnˈlɪtəreri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非文学的，非书面的</td><td>nonliterary usage<br>非文学用法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Dictionaries now record many nonliterary spoken forms.  词典现在收录了许多非文学的口语形式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vulgar</td><td>/ˈvʌlɡər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗俗的，无教养的</td><td>vulgar display of wealth<br>粗俗的炫富</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their vulgar display of wealth embarrassed even their closest friends.  他们粗俗的炫富让连最亲近的朋友都感到难堪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bookish</td><td>/ˈbʊkɪʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>书卷气的，书面语的</td><td>a bookish phrase<br>书面语气的措辞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His bookish vocabulary sounded odd in a casual chat.  他书卷气的用词在闲聊中显得别扭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 formal</td><td>/ˈfɔːrməl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>正式的，规范的</td><td>formal written English<br>正式书面英语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Use formal language when writing to the committee.  给委员会写信时要用正式语言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 learned</td><td>/ˈlɜːrnɪd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>博学的，有学问的</td><td>a learned professor<br>博学的教授</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The learned judge quoted three languages in a single short opinion.  这位博学的法官在一份简短意见中引用了三种语言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 literary</td><td>/ˈlɪtəreri/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>文学的，书面的</td><td>literary style<br>文学文体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The word survives only in literary contexts today.  这个词今天只存在于文学语境中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>versatile</b></td><td>/ˈvɜːrsətəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>多才多艺的，全能的</td><td>a versatile performer<br>多才多艺的表演者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A versatile performer, she sings, dances, and plays three instruments.  作为多才多艺的表演者，她能唱歌、跳舞并演奏三种乐器。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adaptable</td><td>/əˈdæptəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>适应力强的；可改编的</td><td>a highly adaptable species<br>适应力极强的物种</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Crows are adaptable birds that thrive even in crowded cities.  乌鸦适应力很强，连拥挤的城市里也能繁盛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 all-around</td><td>/ˌɔːl əˈraʊnd/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>全面的，多才多艺的</td><td>an all-around athlete<br>全能运动员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is an all-around student who excels in sports and music.  她是个全面发展的学生，体育和音乐都很出色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ambidextrous</td><td>/ˌæmbəˈdekstrəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>双手同利的；多才多艺的</td><td>an ambidextrous pitcher<br>双手都能投的投手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Being ambidextrous let him write while holding the phone.  双手同样灵巧让他能一边打电话一边写字。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 protean</td><td>/ˈproʊtiən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>善变的；多才多艺的</td><td>a protean talent<br>多变的才华</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The actor's protean talent let him play villains and saints equally well.  这位演员多变的才华使他演反派和圣人都同样出色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 universal</td><td>/ˌjuːnəˈvɜːrsəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>普遍的，全体的</td><td>universal human rights<br>普世人权</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Music seems to have a universal appeal across all cultures.  音乐似乎在所有文化中都有普遍的感染力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 limited</td><td>/ˈlɪmɪtɪd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有限的，受限制的</td><td>limited resources<br>有限的资源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 With limited time we could visit only two of the temples.  时间有限，我们只能参观其中两座寺庙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>verse</b></td><td>/vɜːrs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使精通，使熟悉；诗歌</td><td>well versed in law<br>精通法律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years abroad versed him thoroughly in the customs of that region.  多年的海外生活使他十分熟悉那个地区的风俗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 acquaint</td><td>/əˈkweɪnt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使熟悉，使了解</td><td>acquaint yourself with the rules<br>熟悉这些规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New employees must acquaint themselves with the safety procedures first.  新员工必须先熟悉安全流程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 familiarize</td><td>/fəˈmɪliəraɪz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使熟悉，使了解</td><td>familiarize yourself with the rules<br>让自己熟悉规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New staff must familiarize themselves with safety procedures.  新员工必须熟悉安全流程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inform</td><td>/ɪnˈfɔːrm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>通知，告知；举报</td><td>inform the police immediately<br>立即通知警方</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please inform me as soon as the results become available.  结果一出来请立刻通知我。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ballad</td><td>/ˈbæləd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>民谣，叙事歌</td><td>an old sea ballad<br>古老的航海民谣</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fisherman sang a ballad his grandfather had taught him.  渔夫唱起祖父教他的一首民谣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lyric</td><td>/ˈlɪrɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>如诗歌般流畅甜美的</td><td>a lyric passage of prose<br>一段流畅优美的散文</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel contains a lyric passage of prose about summer rain.  这部小说中有一段关于夏雨的抒情散文。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 poetry</td><td>/ˈpoʊətri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>诗歌，诗作</td><td>write poetry<br>写诗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She read modern poetry aloud every Sunday evening.  每个周日傍晚她都朗读现代诗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 song</td><td>/ˈsɔːŋ/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>歌，歌曲</td><td>a folk song<br>民歌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole hall joined in the final song.  全场一起唱起了最后一首歌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 misinform</td><td>/ˌmɪsɪnˈfɔːrm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>误告，提供错误信息</td><td>misinform the public<br>误导公众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Someone misinformed us about the museum's closing time last Saturday.  上周六有人把博物馆的关门时间告错了我们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mislead</td><td>/mɪsˈliːd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>误导，使误信</td><td>mislead investors<br>误导投资者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The chart was designed to mislead careless readers.  这张图表意在误导不细心的读者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prose</td><td>/ˈproʊz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>散文；平淡的文字</td><td>clear simple prose<br>清晰朴素的散文</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He writes plain prose rather than flowery verse.  他写朴实的散文，而不是华丽的诗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 2

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>vertigo</b></td><td>/ˈvɜːrtɪɡoʊ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>眩晕</td><td>suffer from vertigo<br>患有眩晕症</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Standing near the glass wall gave him a sudden attack of vertigo.  站在玻璃墙边让他突然一阵眩晕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dizziness</td><td>/ˈdɪzinəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>头晕，眩晕</td><td>sudden dizziness<br>突然头晕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Standing up too fast caused a wave of dizziness.  起身太快引起一阵头晕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 giddiness</td><td>/ˈɡɪdinəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>头晕，眩晕</td><td>a fit of giddiness<br>一阵眩晕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The heat and noise together brought on real giddiness.  高温加噪音让人真的眩晕起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sobriety</td><td>/səˈbraɪəti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>清醒节制；严肃</td><td>a mood of sobriety<br>严肃的气氛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He has kept his sobriety for more than ten years.  他已保持戒酒清醒十多年了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>verve</b></td><td>/vɜːrv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>活力，热情</td><td>play with verve<br>充满激情地演奏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young pianist played that difficult passage with remarkable verve.  这位年轻钢琴家以非凡的热情演奏了那段高难度乐章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 animation</td><td>/ˌænəˈmeɪʃən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>生气，活力</td><td>speak with animation<br>兴致勃勃地讲话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She described the trip with unusual animation and plenty of gestures.  她异常兴致勃勃地描述了那次旅行，还比划了许多手势。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dynamism</td><td>/ˈdaɪnəˌmɪzəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>活力，干劲</td><td>economic dynamism<br>经济活力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city's dynamism attracts young workers every year.  这座城市的活力每年吸引年轻劳动者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 energy</td><td>/ˈenərdʒi/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>精力，活力；能量</td><td>full of restless energy<br>充满旺盛的精力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children still had plenty of energy after the long hike.  长途徒步之后孩子们仍精力充沛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exuberance</td><td>/ɪɡˈzuːbərəns/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>热情洋溢，兴高采烈</td><td>youthful exuberance<br>年轻人的热情洋溢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The band played with an exuberance that lifted the whole room.  乐队演奏得热情洋溢，带动了满屋气氛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 liveliness</td><td>/ˈlaɪvlinəs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>活力，生气</td><td>the liveliness of the market<br>市场的热闹活力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The liveliness of the class surprised the visiting teacher.  班上的活力让来访的老师惊讶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 robustness</td><td>/roʊˈbʌstnəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>强健，稳健</td><td>the robustness of the design<br>设计的稳健性</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Engineers praised the robustness of the old bridge.  工程师赞赏这座旧桥的稳固。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vibrancy</td><td>/ˈvaɪbrənsi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>活力，生机</td><td>urban vibrancy<br>城市的活力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New cafes restored some vibrancy to the old harbor.  新开的咖啡馆让老码头恢复了些活力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 vigorousness</del></td><td>/ˈvɪɡərəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>精力充沛，强健</td><td>surprising vigorousness<br>出人意料的精力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 At eighty he kept a vigorousness that shamed younger men.  八十岁的他仍精力充沛，让年轻人自愧不如。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vim</td><td>/vɪm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>活力，精力</td><td>full of vim and vigor<br>精力充沛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 At seventy he still worked with surprising vim and good humor.  七十岁的他干活仍精力惊人，心情愉快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vitality</td><td>/vaɪˈtæləti/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>活力，生命力</td><td>brim with vitality<br>充满活力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old market still brims with vitality on early summer mornings.  夏日清晨，这座老市场依然充满活力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lethargy</td><td>/ˈleθərdʒi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>无生气，倦怠，昏睡</td><td>shake off his lethargy<br>摆脱他的倦怠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A short walk helped him shake off his afternoon lethargy.  短短的散步帮他摆脱了午后的倦怠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 listlessness</td><td>/ˈlɪstləsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>倦怠，无精打采</td><td>an air of listlessness<br>一副无精打采的样子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A strange listlessness settled over the office in August.  八月里办公室弥漫着一种奇怪的倦怠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sluggishness</td><td>/ˈslʌɡɪʃnəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>迟缓，无精打采</td><td>morning sluggishness<br>早晨的迟缓无力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The heat explained the general sluggishness of the crew.  炎热解释了船员们普遍的无精打采。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 torpidity</del></td><td>/tɔːrˈpɪdəti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>迟钝，麻木</td><td>sink into torpidity<br>陷入麻木迟钝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Winter torpidity kept the frogs buried in the mud.  冬日的麻木状态让青蛙埋在泥中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>venue</b></td><td>/ˈvenjuː/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>场地</td><td>change the venue<br>更换场地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Organizers changed the venue after the hall roof began to leak.  礼堂屋顶开始漏水后，组织者更换了场地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 locus</td><td>/ˈloʊkəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>地点，中心位置</td><td>the locus of power<br>权力的中心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The port became the locus of all regional trade.  港口成了整个区域贸易的中心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 position</td><td>/pəˈzɪʃən/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>位置，方位</td><td>a central position<br>居中的位置</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tent was pitched in an exposed position on the ridge.  帐篷搭在山脊上一处暴露的位置。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 site</td><td>/ˈsaɪt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>地点，场址</td><td>a building site<br>建筑工地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Archaeologists mapped the site before any digging began.  开挖之前考古学家先测绘了这处场址。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spot</td><td>/ˈspɑːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>污点，斑点；地点</td><td>a spot on the shirt<br>衬衫上的污点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A grease spot on the sleeve ruined his only white shirt.  袖子上的油污弄坏了他唯一的白衬衫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 place</td><td>/ˈpleɪs/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>提出（问题）；放置</td><td>place the issue before them<br>把问题提交给他们</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She placed the question before the committee that morning.  那天上午她把这个问题提交给了委员会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>vestige</b></td><td>/ˈvestɪdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>微小的遗迹，小痕迹</td><td>the last vestige of hope<br>最后一丝希望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a vestige of the ancient wall remains beside the modern road.  现代公路旁只剩下古城墙的一点遗迹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 echo</td><td>/ˈekoʊ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>附和，重复；回声</td><td>echo his opinion<br>附和他的意见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Younger delegates simply echoed the governor's opinion.  年轻代表只是附和州长的意见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ghost</td><td>/ˈɡoʊst/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>残迹，一丝痕迹</td><td>the ghost of a smile<br>一丝淡淡的笑意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only the ghost of the old inscription remained on the stone.  石上只剩下旧铭文的一点残痕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 relic</td><td>/ˈrelɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>遗物，遗迹</td><td>a relic of the past<br>过去的遗物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The wooden wheel is a relic of the village's first mill.  这个木轮是村里第一座磨坊的遗物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 remain</td><td>/rɪˈmeɪn/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>保持不变，留存</td><td>remain unchanged for years<br>多年保持不变</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old rules remain in force despite the new law.  尽管有了新法，旧规则仍然有效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 remnant</td><td>/ˈremnənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>残余，剩余物</td><td>the last remnants of<br>…最后的残余</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a few remnants of the ancient wall still stand today.  今天只剩下古城墙的少量残余仍然矗立。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 residual</td><td>/rɪˈzɪdʒuəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>剩余的，残存的</td><td>residual traces of paint<br>残存的颜料痕迹</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Residual damp in the wall ruined the new plaster.  墙里残存的湿气毁了新抹的灰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shadow</td><td>/ˈʃædoʊ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>偷偷尾随</td><td>shadow a suspect<br>偷偷尾随嫌疑人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two detectives agreed to shadow the suspect through the crowded market.  两名侦探决定在拥挤的市场里偷偷尾随嫌疑人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trace</td><td>/ˈtreɪs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>痕迹；微量</td><td>a trace of irony<br>一丝讽意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tea showed only a trace of bitterness.  这茶只有一丝苦味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 incomplete</td><td>/ˌɪnkəmˈpliːt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不完全的，未完成的</td><td>an incomplete list<br>不完整的清单</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The survey remains incomplete because two districts never returned their forms.  调查仍不完整，因为两个区从未交回表格。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rudimentary</td><td>/ˌruːdɪˈmentri/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>初始的，未发展的；最根本的，基础的</td><td>rudimentary knowledge<br>基础的知识</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He had only a rudimentary knowledge of chemistry when he joined the lab.  他加入实验室时对化学只有基础的了解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 undeveloped</td><td>/ˌʌndɪˈveləpt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>未开发的，未成熟的</td><td>undeveloped land<br>未开发的土地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Much of the island remains undeveloped and hard to reach.  岛上大部分地区仍未开发，难以到达。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 adult</td><td>/əˈdʌlt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>成年的，成熟的；成年人</td><td>an adult attitude<br>成熟的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She handled the crisis in a calm, adult way.  她以冷静成熟的方式处理了这场危机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 full-blown</td><td>/ˌfʊlˈbloʊn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>完全成熟的，全面爆发的</td><td>a full-blown crisis<br>全面爆发的危机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A minor dispute grew into a full-blown lawsuit.  一场小争执演变成全面的诉讼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 full-fledged</td><td>/ˌfʊl ˈfledʒd/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>完全成熟的，羽翼已成的</td><td>a full-fledged member<br>正式成员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After three years she became a full-fledged engineer.  三年后她成为一名完全合格的工程师。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 matured</td><td>/məˈtʃʊrd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>成熟的，长成的</td><td>a matured cheese<br>熟成的奶酪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The matured wine sold for four times the price of new bottles.  熟成的酒卖出了新酒四倍的价钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ripe</td><td>/ˈraɪp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>成熟的，熟透的；时机成熟的</td><td>ripe for change<br>变革时机成熟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The peaches are finally ripe enough to pick and eat.  这些桃子终于熟到可以采下来吃了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ripened</td><td>/ˈraɪpənd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>成熟的，熟透的</td><td>ripened fruit<br>熟透的水果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Ripened peaches filled the crates by early August.  八月初板条箱里装满了熟透的桃子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>vocation</b></td><td>/voʊˈkeɪʃn/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>职业</td><td>choose a vocation<br>选择职业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was lucky to choose a vocation that matched his hobby.  他很幸运，选择了与自己爱好一致的职业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 calling</td><td>/ˈkɔːlɪŋ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>职业，使命</td><td>follow one's calling<br>追随自己的使命</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Teaching was a calling for her, not merely a job.  教书对她是使命，而不只是工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 employment</td><td>/emˈplɔɪmənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>工作，就业</td><td>find employment<br>找到工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He found employment at the shipyard within a week.  他一周内在船厂找到了工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 occupation</td><td>/ˌɑːkjəˈpeɪʃən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>职业，工作</td><td>state your occupation<br>填写你的职业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her occupation was listed simply as engineer.  她的职业只写作工程师。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 profession</td><td>/prəˈfeʃən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>专业，职业</td><td>the medical profession<br>医疗行业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Law was the only profession his family respected.  法律是他家中唯一看重的职业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trade</td><td>/ˈtreɪd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>贸易；交换，交易</td><td>trade with China<br>与中国进行贸易</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two towns traded salt and wool for many centuries.  这两个镇几个世纪以来用盐交换羊毛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 work</td><td>/ˈwɜːrk/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>劳作，工作；作品</td><td>hard physical work<br>繁重的体力劳动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A day of heavy work in the fields left them exhausted.  在田里干了一天重活，他们累得不行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hobby</td><td>/ˈhɑːbi/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>业余爱好</td><td>a weekend hobby<br>周末爱好</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Woodworking started as a hobby and became his living.  木工起初是爱好，后来成了他的生计。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>veteran</b></td><td>/ˈvetərən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>老兵；有丰富经验的人</td><td>a veteran journalist<br>资深记者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A veteran journalist, she has covered four wars and six elections.  作为资深记者，她报道过四场战争和六次选举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 doyen</td><td>/ˈdɔɪən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>资深人士，元老</td><td>the doyen of critics<br>批评界的元老</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 For thirty years he was the doyen of critics at that newspaper.  三十年来他一直是那家报纸批评界的元老。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 expert</td><td>/ˈekspərt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>专家；熟练的，专业的</td><td>a leading expert<br>顶尖专家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They called in an expert to examine the cracked foundation.  他们请来专家检查开裂的地基。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 master</td><td>/ˈmæstər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>大师，名家；主人</td><td>a master of disguise<br>伪装大师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a master of the craft could carve such fine details.  只有行家高手才能雕出这般精细的细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 maven</td><td>/ˈmeɪvn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>专家</td><td>a marketing maven<br>营销专家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magazine hired a marketing maven to explain why the campaign had failed.  杂志请来一位营销专家解释这次宣传为何失败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 warhorse</td><td>/ˈwɔrhɔrs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>老将，老手</td><td>an old political warhorse<br>政坛老将</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The party sent in an old warhorse to steady the campaign.  该党派了一位老将来稳住选战。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 accomplished</td><td>/əˈkɑːmplɪʃt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>技艺精湛的，有造诣的</td><td>an accomplished pianist<br>技艺精湛的钢琴家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is an accomplished pianist who has toured three continents.  她是位技艺精湛的钢琴家，已在三大洲巡演。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adept</td><td>/əˈdept/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>内行的，熟练的；行家</td><td>adept at negotiation<br>擅长谈判</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is remarkably adept at calming angry customers.  她极擅长安抚愤怒的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 consummate</td><td>/ˈkɑːnsəmət/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>专业的，有造诣的；无纰漏的，完美的</td><td>a consummate professional<br>极为专业的行家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She handled the hostile interview with the ease of a consummate diplomat.  她像老练的外交家一样从容应对了这场充满敌意的采访。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 experienced</td><td>/ɪkˈspɪriənst/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有经验的，老练的</td><td>an experienced guide<br>经验丰富的导游</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We hired an experienced nurse to care for my grandmother.  我们请了一位有经验的护士照顾我祖母。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 masterful</td><td>/ˈmæstərfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>技艺精湛的，娴熟的</td><td>a masterful performance<br>一场技艺精湛的演出</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her masterful handling of the violin silenced the whole hall.  她精湛的小提琴技艺让全场肃静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 professed</td><td>/prəˈfest/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>自称的，公开声称的</td><td>a professed expert<br>自称的专家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The professed authority could not answer a basic question.  这位自称的权威连基本问题都答不上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 skilled</td><td>/ˈskɪld/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>熟练的，有技能的</td><td>skilled labor<br>熟练劳动力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The factory struggles to find skilled workers for its new assembly line.  工厂难以为新装配线找到熟练工人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 versed</td><td>/vɜːrst/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>熟知的；精通的</td><td>well versed in history<br>精通历史</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is well versed in both classical and modern poetry.  他对古典诗歌和现代诗歌都很精通。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 virtuoso</td><td>/ˌvɜːrtʃuˈoʊsoʊ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>技艺大师，演奏名家；技艺精湛的</td><td>a violin virtuoso<br>小提琴演奏大师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The violin virtuoso played the difficult passage without a single mistake.  那位小提琴大师毫无差错地演奏了这段难曲。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 beginner</td><td>/bɪˈɡɪnər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>初学者，新手</td><td>a complete beginner<br>完全的新手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This course is designed for the absolute beginner.  这门课程是为完全的新手设计的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 colt</td><td>/ˈkoʊlt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>新手，生手</td><td>an untried colt<br>没经验的新手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team's colts learned fast under the veteran captain.  在老队长带领下队里的新手学得很快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fledgling</td><td>/ˈfledʒlɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>新手</td><td>a fledgling company<br>新兴的公司</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fledgling airline struggled through its very first winter.  这家新兴航空公司艰难度过了第一个冬天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 freshman</td><td>/ˈfreʃmən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>大一新生；新手</td><td>a freshman senator<br>新任参议员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a freshman he knew almost nobody on campus.  作为大一新生，他在校园里几乎谁也不认识。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 greenhorn</td><td>/ˈɡriːnhɔrn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>新手，生手</td><td>a complete greenhorn<br>十足的新手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a greenhorn he asked questions the veterans found obvious.  作为新手，他问的问题老手们觉得显而易见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 neophyte</td><td>/ˈniːəfaɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>初学者，新手</td><td>a neophyte in politics<br>政界的新手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a neophyte in politics, he underestimated how slowly legislation actually moves.  作为政界的新手，他低估了立法进程实际有多缓慢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 novice</td><td>/ˈnɑːvɪs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>新手，初学者</td><td>a complete novice<br>彻底的新手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A complete novice, he had never held a chisel before that afternoon.  他是个彻底的新手，那天下午之前从没握过凿子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 recruit</td><td>/rəˈkruːt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>新成员，新兵；招募</td><td>a raw recruit<br>毫无经验的新兵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Every new recruit spends six weeks in basic training.  每名新兵都要接受六周基础训练。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rookie</td><td>/ˈrʊki/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>新兵；新手；菜鸟</td><td>a rookie mistake<br>新手才犯的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rookie made a nervous throw that cost his team the game.  那名新手一次紧张的传球让球队输掉了比赛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tenderfoot</td><td>/ˈtendərfʊt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>新手，生手</td><td>a mountain tenderfoot<br>登山新手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a tenderfoot he packed twice the gear he needed.  作为新手，他带了两倍于所需的装备。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tyro</td><td>/ˈtaɪroʊ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>新手，业余爱好者</td><td>a tyro at chess<br>国际象棋新手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a tyro at chess, he lost every game that afternoon.  作为国际象棋新手，他那天下午每局都输了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 amateur</td><td>/ˈæməˌtɜːr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>业余爱好者；外行的</td><td>a talented amateur<br>有天赋的业余者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The comet was first spotted by an amateur astronomer.  这颗彗星最先由一位业余天文学家发现。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inexperienced</td><td>/ˌɪnɪkˈspɪriənst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>缺乏经验的，不熟练的</td><td>an inexperienced driver<br>缺乏经验的司机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team was young and inexperienced but full of energy.  这支队伍年轻缺乏经验，却充满活力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inexpert</td><td>/ˌɪnˈekspɜːrt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不熟练的，外行的</td><td>inexpert hands<br>不熟练的手法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 To inexpert eyes the two paintings looked exactly the same.  在外行眼里，这两幅画看起来完全一样。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unprofessional</td><td>/ˌʌnprəˈfeʃənəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不专业的，外行的</td><td>unprofessional conduct<br>不专业的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Arriving late to every meeting looks deeply unprofessional.  每次会议都迟到显得极不专业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unseasoned</td><td>/ʌnˈsizənd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>缺乏经验的；未调味的</td><td>unseasoned recruits<br>毫无经验的新兵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team fielded unseasoned players against the reigning champions.  球队派出毫无经验的球员迎战卫冕冠军。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unskilled</td><td>/ənˈskɪld/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不熟练的，无技术的</td><td>unskilled labor<br>非技术劳工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most of these jobs are unskilled and pay barely above the minimum.  这些工作多为非技术岗，薪水刚过最低标准。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unskillful</td><td>/ʌnˈskɪlfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不熟练的，技艺拙劣的</td><td>unskillful workmanship<br>拙劣的手工活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unskillful repair left the table wobbling badly.  这次拙劣的修理让桌子摇晃得厉害。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>veto</b></td><td>/ˈviːtoʊ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>否决，禁止</td><td>veto the proposed bill<br>否决拟议的法案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The governor promised to veto the bill if it reached his desk.  州长承诺，若这项法案送到他手上就予以否决。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blackball</td><td>/ˈblækˌbɔːl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>投票反对，排斥</td><td>blackball a candidate<br>投票排斥某候选人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two members blackballed him at the club election.  俱乐部选举中两名成员投票否决了他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 decline</td><td>/dɪˈklaɪn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>拒绝；下降，衰退</td><td>decline an invitation<br>谢绝邀请</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She politely declined the invitation to speak at the ceremony.  她礼貌地谢绝了在典礼上发言的邀请。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disallow</td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈlaʊ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>不许，驳回</td><td>disallow the goal<br>判进球无效</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The referee disallowed the goal for an obvious offside.  裁判因明显越位判该进球无效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disapprove</td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈpruːv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>不赞成，反对</td><td>disapprove of the plan<br>不赞成该计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her parents strongly disapprove of the plan to drop out.  她父母强烈不赞成退学的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 kill</td><td>/ˈkɪl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>否决，取消（议案）</td><td>kill the bill<br>否决议案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The senate killed the bill on its second reading.  参议院在二读时否决了这项议案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 negative</td><td>/ˈneɡətɪv/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>否决，反驳；负面的</td><td>negative the proposal<br>否决这项提案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee negatived the motion after a short debate.  委员会经短暂辩论后否决了该动议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 refuse</td><td>/rɪˈfjuːz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>拒绝，不肯；废弃物</td><td>refuse to cooperate<br>拒绝合作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness refused to answer any further questions in court.  证人拒绝在法庭上回答任何进一步的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reject</td><td>/rɪˈdʒekt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>拒绝，驳回；废品</td><td>reject the proposal<br>否决提案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee voted to reject the proposal without further discussion.  委员会投票否决了该提案，不再讨论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 accredit</td><td>/əˈkredɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>认可，授权；归功于</td><td>accredit the university<br>认可该大学资质</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The board finally accredited the new nursing program.  委员会终于认可了这个新的护理专业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 approbate</del></td><td>/ˈæprəbeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞许，认可</td><td>approbate the decision<br>认可这一决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The board approbated the merger after a long private debate.  经过长时间闭门辩论，董事会认可了这次合并。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 authorize</td><td>/ˈɔːθəˌraɪz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>授权，批准，委托</td><td>authorize the payment<br>批准这笔付款</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only the director can authorize spending above that limit.  只有主管能批准超出该限额的开支。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 clear</td><td>/ˈklɪr/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明确的，确凿的；清楚的</td><td>a clear majority<br>明显的多数</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The party won by a clear majority of votes.  该党以明显多数票获胜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 confirm</td><td>/kənˈfɜːrm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>证实，确认，进一步确定</td><td>confirm the findings<br>证实这些发现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Later tests confirmed what the first team had suspected.  后来的检测证实了第一个团队的猜测。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 finalize</td><td>/ˈfaɪnəˌlaɪz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>最终确定，敲定</td><td>finalize the agreement<br>敲定协议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Both sides hope to finalize the agreement before December.  双方希望在十二月前敲定协议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 formalize</td><td>/ˈfɔːrməˌlaɪz/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使正式化，使规范化</td><td>formalize the agreement<br>使协议正式化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two firms decided to formalize their loose partnership.  两家公司决定把松散的合作正式化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ratify</td><td>/ˈrætɪfaɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（官方地）认可，批准</td><td>ratify the peace treaty<br>批准和平条约</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The senate voted to ratify the peace treaty after weeks of debate.  经过数周辩论，参议院投票批准了和平条约。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sanction</td><td>/ˈsæŋkʃn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>批准，同意，认可</td><td>sanction the new policy<br>批准这项新政策</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The board refused to sanction the plan without a detailed budget.  董事会拒绝在没有详细预算的情况下批准该计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 warrant</td><td>/ˈwɔːrənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>保证，担保；使有正当理由</td><td>warrant the quality<br>保证质量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The factory warrants the quality of every tool it sells abroad.  这家工厂为其出口的每件工具的质量提供保证。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>vex</b></td><td>/veks/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使烦恼，使恼怒</td><td>vex the teacher<br>使老师恼怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Constant interruptions vexed the teacher throughout the long afternoon lesson.  不断的打扰让老师在漫长的下午课上十分恼怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aggravate</td><td>/ˈæɡrəveɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>加重，恶化；激怒</td><td>aggravate the situation<br>使局势恶化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Running on the injury will only aggravate it.  带着伤跑步只会让伤势加重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 annoy</td><td>/əˈnɔɪ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使恼怒，不断烦扰</td><td>annoy the neighbors<br>不断打扰邻居</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The barking dog began to annoy every neighbor on the quiet street.  那只不停犬吠的狗开始烦扰这条安静街上的每位邻居。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bother</td><td>/ˈbɑːðər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>打扰，使烦恼；费心</td><td>don't bother me<br>别烦我</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The constant noise from the street really bothers me.  街上不停的噪音真让我烦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exasperate</td><td>/ɪɡˈzæspəreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使恼怒，激怒</td><td>exasperate his parents<br>使他的父母恼火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His constant lateness exasperated even the most patient teachers.  他总是迟到，连最有耐心的老师都被激怒了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gall</td><td>/ɡɔːl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（使）焦躁，激怒</td><td>galled by the remark<br>被那句话惹恼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His smug tone galled everyone sitting around the conference table.  他自鸣得意的语气激怒了围坐在会议桌旁的每个人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grate</td><td>/ɡreɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>刮擦（以发出刺耳的声音）；骚扰，惹恼</td><td>grate on my nerves<br>让我心烦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her constant humming began to grate on everyone in the small office.  她不停的哼唱开始让小办公室里的每个人心烦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 irk</td><td>/ɜːrk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使烦恼，使厌倦</td><td>irk his colleagues<br>使同事厌烦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His habit of humming while working irked everyone in the office.  他工作时哼歌的习惯使办公室里所有人都很厌烦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nettle</td><td>/ˈnetl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>惹怒</td><td>nettled by criticism<br>被批评惹怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was clearly nettled by the reporter's rude question.  记者无礼的提问显然惹恼了她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peeve</td><td>/piːv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>打扰，惹恼</td><td>peeve the audience<br>惹恼观众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Loud phone conversations on the train really peeve the other passengers.  火车上大声打电话确实惹恼其他乘客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rile</td><td>/raɪl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>刺激，惹怒</td><td>rile up the crowd<br>激怒人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing riles him more than being interrupted in the middle of a sentence.  没有什么比话说到一半被打断更让他恼火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 appease</td><td>/əˈpiːz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安抚，平息；满足</td><td>appease the angry crowd<br>安抚愤怒的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offered refunds to appease its angry customers.  公司提供退款以安抚愤怒的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 assuage</td><td>/əˈsweɪdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>缓和，减轻；平息</td><td>assuage his fears<br>减轻他的恐惧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor's speech did little to assuage public anger.  市长的演讲几乎没能平息公众的怒火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conciliate</td><td>/kənˈsɪlieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安抚，平息；调解</td><td>conciliate angry customers<br>安抚愤怒的顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager tried to conciliate the angry customers with refunds.  经理试图用退款来安抚愤怒的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mollify</td><td>/ˈmɑːlɪfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>平息，抚慰，缓和</td><td>mollify an angry customer<br>安抚愤怒的顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sincere apology from the manager mollified the furious client.  经理诚挚的道歉安抚了这位愤怒的客户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 placate</td><td>/ˈpleɪkeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（通过让步以）平息抚慰</td><td>placate the critics<br>平息批评者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offered full refunds to placate its angriest customers.  公司提供全额退款以平息最愤怒的客户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 propitiate</td><td>/prəˈpɪʃieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抚慰，劝解</td><td>propitiate the angry gods<br>抚慰愤怒的神灵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Villagers offered rice and flowers to propitiate the mountain spirits.  村民献上米和花来抚慰山神。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>viable</b></td><td>/ˈvaɪəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可行的</td><td>a viable alternative<br>可行的替代方案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Solar power is now a viable alternative for many remote villages.  太阳能如今对许多偏远村庄是可行的替代方案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 achievable</td><td>/əˈtʃiːvəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可实现的，能达到的</td><td>an achievable goal<br>可实现的目标</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Set achievable targets for the first three months.  为头三个月设定可实现的目标。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 attainable</td><td>/əˈteɪnəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可达到的，可获得的</td><td>an attainable standard<br>可达到的标准</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new grade is attainable with steady weekly practice.  只要每周稳定练习，新等级是可以达到的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 doable</td><td>/ˈduːəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>做得到的，可行的</td><td>perfectly doable<br>完全做得到</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Finishing by Friday is doable if two more join us.  再来两人的话周五完工是做得到的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 feasible</td><td>/ˈfiːzəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可行的，行得通的</td><td>a feasible plan<br>可行的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Engineers judged the tunnel feasible but very costly.  工程师认为隧道可行，但造价极高。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 practicable</td><td>/ˈpræktəkəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可实行的，行得通的</td><td>practicable measures<br>可实行的措施</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Repairs must begin as soon as it is practicable.  一旦条件允许就必须开始维修。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 realizable</td><td>/ˌriːəˈlaɪzəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可实现的</td><td>a realizable ambition<br>可实现的抱负</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 With a scholarship her plan became realizable at last.  有了奖学金她的计划终于可以实现。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 workable</td><td>/ˈwɜːrkəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可行的，行之有效的</td><td>a workable compromise<br>行得通的妥协</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They reached a workable arrangement about the shared yard.  他们就共用院子达成了可行的安排。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hopeless</td><td>/ˈhoʊpləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无望的，绝望的</td><td>a hopeless situation<br>毫无希望的局面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The doctors described his condition as sadly hopeless.  医生们说他的病情令人遗憾地毫无希望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 impossible</td><td>/ˌɪmˈpɑːsəbəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可能的</td><td>an impossible demand<br>不可能的要求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Repairing the roof in one day proved impossible.  一天修好屋顶最终证明不可能。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 impracticable</td><td>/ˌɪmˈpræktɪkəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>行不通的，难以实行的</td><td>an impracticable scheme<br>行不通的方案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The plan was impracticable without a second bridge.  没有第二座桥这个计划行不通。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 infeasible</td><td>/ˌɪnˈfiːzəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可行的</td><td>technically infeasible<br>技术上不可行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The deadline made the whole design infeasible.  这个期限使整个设计变得不可行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unattainable</td><td>/ˌʌnəˈteɪnəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无法达到的</td><td>an unattainable ideal<br>无法达到的理想</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Perfect fairness may be an unattainable ideal.  完全的公平也许是无法达到的理想。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unviable</td><td>/ʌnˈvaɪəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可行的，难以存续的</td><td>an unviable business<br>难以存续的生意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rising rents made the little bookshop unviable.  房租上涨让这家小书店难以维持。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unworkable</td><td>/ʌnˈwɜːrkəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>行不通的，无法实施的</td><td>an unworkable rule<br>无法实施的规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Staff called the new schedule completely unworkable.  员工称新排班表完全行不通。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>vicarious</b></td><td>/vaɪˈkeriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>间接感受的；代理的</td><td>vicarious pleasure<br>间接得到的乐趣</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Parents often take vicarious pleasure in their children's small victories.  父母常从孩子的小胜利中获得间接的快乐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indirect</td><td>/ˌɪndəˈrekt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迂回的，间接的</td><td>an indirect route<br>迂回的路线</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We took an indirect route to avoid the flooded highway.  我们走了绕行路线以避开被淹的公路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 substitute</td><td>/ˈsʌbstɪtuːt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>取代者，替代品</td><td>a poor substitute for milk<br>牛奶的劣质替代品</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Powdered milk is a poor substitute for the fresh kind.  奶粉是鲜奶的劣质替代品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 surrogate</td><td>/ˈsɜːrəɡət/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>替代品</td><td>a surrogate parent<br>代理家长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The kindly neighbor became a surrogate parent to the orphaned twins.  好心的邻居成了这对孤儿双胞胎的代理家长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 firsthand</td><td>/ˌfɜːrstˈhænd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>直接的，第一手的</td><td>firsthand experience<br>第一手经验</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She reported the flood firsthand from the riverbank.  她在河岸边作了洪水的第一手报道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 3

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>vicious</b></td><td>/ˈvɪʃəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>凶恶的，恶毒的</td><td>a vicious circle<br>恶性循环</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dog gave a vicious bite that required several stitches.  那只狗凶恶地咬了一口，需要缝几针。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 atrocious</td><td>/əˈtroʊʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极坏的，极其残忍的</td><td>an atrocious crime<br>残暴的罪行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court described the killing as an atrocious crime.  法庭称这起杀人事件是极其残忍的罪行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 barbaric</td><td>/bɑrˈbærɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>野蛮的，残暴的</td><td>barbaric cruelty<br>野蛮的残酷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The treatment of prisoners there was frankly barbaric.  那里对囚犯的待遇坦白说是野蛮的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 barbarous</td><td>/ˈbɑːrbərəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>野蛮的，凶残的</td><td>barbarous cruelty<br>野蛮的残忍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The invaders were remembered for their barbarous treatment of prisoners.  侵略者因对俘虏的野蛮对待而被人记住。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brutal</td><td>/ˈbruːtəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>残忍的；直白无情的</td><td>a brutal attack<br>残忍的袭击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prisoners described the brutal treatment they received in the camp.  囚犯们描述了他们在营中受到的残忍对待。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 butcherly</del></td><td>/ˈbʊtʃərli/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>残忍的，屠夫般的</td><td>a butcherly assault<br>残忍的攻击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Witnesses described the raid as butcherly and senseless.  证人称这次袭击残忍且毫无意义。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fiendish</td><td>/ˈfiːndɪʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶魔般的，恶毒的；极难的</td><td>a fiendish plot<br>恶毒的阴谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The detective uncovered a fiendish plan to poison the water supply.  侦探揭露了一个恶毒的投毒供水计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heartless</td><td>/ˈhɑːrtləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无情的，狠心的</td><td>a heartless decision<br>无情的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was heartless to fire her the week before Christmas.  在圣诞节前一周解雇她真是无情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inhumane</td><td>/ˌɪnhjuˈmeɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不人道的，残忍的</td><td>inhumane treatment of prisoners<br>对囚犯的残忍对待</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics condemned the inhumane conditions inside the overcrowded shelter.  批评者谴责了过度拥挤的收容所内不人道的条件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sadistic</td><td>/səˈdɪstɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>残虐的，以施虐为乐的</td><td>a sadistic pleasure<br>施虐般的快感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He took a sadistic pleasure in reading out every low score.  他念出每个低分时有一种施虐般的快感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 savage</td><td>/ˈsævədʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>凶残的，野蛮的；猛烈攻击</td><td>a savage beating<br>野蛮的殴打</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics savaged the film for its careless treatment of history.  批评家猛烈批评该片对历史的草率处理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 truculent</td><td>/ˈtrʌkjələnt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>好战的，好斗的；粗野的</td><td>a truculent response<br>咄咄逼人的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The truculent teenager argued with every teacher who tried to correct him.  这个好斗的少年跟每位试图纠正他的老师都吵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 benign</td><td>/bɪˈnaɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的</td><td>a benign influence<br>有益无害的影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The change in policy proved benign for most small farmers.  事实证明，政策变化对大多数小农户是无害的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 benignant</del></td><td>/bɪˈnɪɡnənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>仁慈的，宽厚的</td><td>a benignant smile<br>慈祥的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old abbot listened with a benignant expression on his face.  老修院长面带慈祥的神情倾听着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
