<tr><td>↳近 profligate</td><td>/ˈprɑːflɪɡət/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>挥金如土的，挥霍的</td><td>profligate spending<br>挥霍无度的开支</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The council's profligate spending on ceremonies angered local taxpayers.  议会在典礼上挥霍无度的开支激怒了当地纳税人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spender</td><td>/ˈspendər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>花钱的人；挥霍者</td><td>a big spender<br>花钱大手的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My uncle is a big spender who never checks his bank balance.  我叔叔花钱大手大脚，从不看银行余额。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spendthrift</td><td>/ˈspendθrɪft/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>挥霍者，败家子</td><td>a reckless spendthrift<br>肆意挥霍的败家子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A reckless spendthrift, he owed money to nearly every friend.  作为一个挥霍无度的人，他几乎欠了每个朋友的钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 squanderer</del></td><td>/ˈskwɑndərər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>浪费者，挥霍者</td><td>a squanderer of talent<br>浪费才华的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 History remembers him as a squanderer of a vast family fortune.  历史把他记作败掉万贯家财的挥霍者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 waster</td><td>/ˈweɪstər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>挥霍者；无用之人</td><td>an idle waster<br>游手好闲的败家子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His father called him a waster who had thrown away every chance.  他父亲说他是个败家子，白白丢掉了每个机会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 economizer</del></td><td>/ɪˈkɑːnəmaɪzər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>节约者，节省用具</td><td>a careful economizer<br>精打细算的节约者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a lifelong economizer, she never wasted a coin.  作为终身节约的人，她从不浪费一分钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 penny-pincher</td><td>/ˈpɛni ˌpɪntʃər/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>吝啬鬼，一分钱都省的人</td><td>a notorious penny-pincher<br>出名的吝啬鬼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His uncle was a penny-pincher who reused envelopes.  他叔叔是个连信封都重复使用的吝啬鬼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>watershed</b></td><td>/ˈwɔːtərʃed/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>重要关头，分水岭</td><td>a watershed moment<br>分水岭般的时刻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That election was a watershed in the history of the republic.  那次选举是这个共和国历史上的分水岭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 climax</td><td>/ˈklaɪˌmæks/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>高潮，顶点</td><td>the climax of the story<br>故事的高潮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film builds slowly toward a violent and unexpected climax.  影片缓慢推向一个暴烈而意外的高潮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 corner</td><td>/ˈkɔːrnər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>角落；转折点</td><td>turn the corner<br>度过难关，出现转机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The economy has finally turned the corner after two hard years.  经过两年艰难时期，经济终于出现转机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 event</td><td>/ɪˈvent/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>事件，大事</td><td>a defining historical event<br>决定性的历史事件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fall of the wall was a defining event of the century.  那道墙的倒塌是该世纪的决定性事件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 landmark</td><td>/ˈlændˌmɑːrk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>里程碑；地标</td><td>a landmark ruling<br>里程碑式的裁决</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court's landmark ruling changed employment law forever.  法院里程碑式的裁决永久改变了劳动法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 milestone</td><td>/ˈmaɪlˌstoʊn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>里程碑，重要阶段</td><td>a major milestone<br>重要的里程碑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her first solo exhibition was a milestone in her career.  她的首次个展是她职业生涯的里程碑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>wax</b></td><td>/wæks/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>增大，增强</td><td>wax and wane<br>盛衰交替，此消彼长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His influence in the party waxed steadily during the long campaign.  在漫长的竞选中，他在党内的影响力稳步增强。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 accelerate</td><td>/əkˈseləreɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>加速，加快</td><td>accelerate the process<br>加快进程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New funding accelerated the development of the vaccine.  新的资金加快了疫苗的研发。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 accumulate</td><td>/əˈkjuːmjəleɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词、及物动词</td><td>逐渐增长，积累</td><td>accumulate wealth<br>积累财富</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Dust had accumulated on the shelves during our absence.  我们离开期间架子上积了灰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 appreciate</td><td>/əˈpriːʃieɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>增值，涨价；欣赏</td><td>appreciate in value<br>价值上涨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old farmhouse has appreciated considerably over the last decade.  这座老农舍在过去十年里大幅增值。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 balloon</td><td>/bəˈluːn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>迅速增加</td><td>costs have ballooned<br>成本迅速膨胀</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Repair costs ballooned from two thousand to twenty thousand dollars.  维修费用从两千美元猛增到两万美元。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 boom</td><td>/ˈbuːm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>繁荣，激增；轰鸣</td><td>an economic boom<br>经济繁荣期</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Tourism boomed after the new airport opened last spring.  新机场去年春天启用后，旅游业迅速繁荣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 burgeon</td><td>/ˈbɜːrdʒən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>迅速成长扩大，蓬勃发展</td><td>a burgeoning market<br>迅速发展的市场</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city's tech sector burgeoned after the new university opened.  新大学开办后，该市的科技产业迅速壮大。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enlarge</td><td>/ɪnˈlɑːrdʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>扩大，增大</td><td>enlarge the photograph<br>放大照片</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They enlarged the kitchen by removing one interior wall.  他们拆掉一道内墙把厨房扩大了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 escalate</td><td>/ˈeskəleɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>（使）（战争等）升级，扩大</td><td>escalate into violence<br>升级为暴力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The border dispute quickly escalated into a full-scale armed conflict.  边界争端迅速升级为全面武装冲突。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 expand</td><td>/ɪkˈspænd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>扩大，扩张；详述</td><td>expand the business<br>扩大业务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company plans to expand into three new markets.  公司计划扩张到三个新市场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 proliferate</td><td>/prəˈlɪfəreɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>快速繁殖；激增</td><td>proliferate rapidly online<br>在网上迅速激增</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cheap smartphones proliferated across the country in just five years.  廉价智能手机在短短五年内遍布全国。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rise</td><td>/ˈraɪz/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>上升，兴起</td><td>the rise of an empire<br>一个帝国的兴起</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Historians still debate the rise of that short-lived empire.  历史学家仍在争论那个短命帝国的兴起。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 contract</td><td>/ˈkɑːnˌtrækt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>契约，合同；收缩；感染疾病</td><td>sign a contract<br>签订合同</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Both parties signed the contract without reading the final clause carefully.  双方都没有仔细读最后一条就签了合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 decrease</td><td>/dɪˈkriːs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>减少，下降</td><td>a sharp decrease<br>急剧下降</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Traffic accidents decreased noticeably after the new speed limits appeared.  新限速出台后交通事故明显减少。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 diminish</td><td>/dɪˈmɪnɪʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>（使）减少；轻视，贬低</td><td>diminish in importance<br>重要性下降</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His influence diminished steadily after he left the board.  他离开董事会后影响力持续减弱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dwindle</td><td>/ˈdwɪndl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>逐渐减少</td><td>dwindle to nothing<br>逐渐减少至无</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their savings dwindled during the months of unemployment.  失业的几个月里他们的积蓄逐渐减少。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lessen</td><td>/ˈlesən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>减少，减轻</td><td>lessen the risk<br>减少风险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Simple exercises lessen the pain in his stiff shoulder.  简单的锻炼减轻了他僵硬肩膀的疼痛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 recede</td><td>/rɪˈsiːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>退去，消退，减弱</td><td>recede into the distance<br>渐渐远去消失</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The floodwaters slowly receded from the village streets.  洪水缓缓从村庄的街道退去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>waylay</b></td><td>/weɪˈleɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>埋伏，伏击</td><td>waylay a traveler<br>伏击旅人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Bandits used to waylay travelers on that narrow mountain road.  过去土匪常在那条狭窄的山路上伏击旅人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ambush</td><td>/ˈæmˌbʊʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>埋伏，伏击</td><td>lie in ambush<br>设伏等候</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rebels ambushed the supply convoy in a narrow valley.  叛军在狭窄的山谷里伏击了补给车队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assault</td><td>/əˈsɔːlt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>攻击，袭击</td><td>launch an assault<br>发动攻击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Troops launched a night assault on the fortress.  部队对要塞发动了夜间进攻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lurk</td><td>/lɜːrk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>潜伏</td><td>lurk in the shadows<br>潜伏在暗处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A photographer lurked in the shadows outside the actress's hotel all night.  一名摄影师整夜潜伏在女演员酒店外的暗处。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>welter</b></td><td>/ˈweltər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>混乱，动乱</td><td>a welter of details<br>杂乱的一堆细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report buried its main point in a welter of small details.  这份报告把要点埋在了杂乱的琐碎细节之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disturbance</td><td>/dɪˈstɜːrbəns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>骚乱，扰乱</td><td>cause a disturbance<br>引起骚乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police were called after a disturbance broke out in the bar.  酒吧里发生骚乱后警察被叫来了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 furor</td><td>/ˈfjʊrɔːr/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>喧闹，狂怒；激动，狂热</td><td>cause a public furor<br>引起公众哗然</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painting caused a public furor when it was first shown.  这幅画首次展出时引起公众哗然。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hurricane</td><td>/ˈhɜːrəkeɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>飓风般的事物，引起动荡的事物</td><td>a hurricane of protest<br>抗议的风暴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A hurricane of protest followed the sudden announcement of the new tax.  新税突然宣布之后，随即掀起一场抗议的风暴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pandemonium</td><td>/ˌpændɪˈmoʊniəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>喧嚣，一片混乱</td><td>pandemonium broke out<br>顿时一片混乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Pandemonium broke out when the final goal was scored.  最后一个进球时全场一片沸腾混乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 turmoil</td><td>/ˈtɜːrmɔɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>混乱，动荡，骚动</td><td>in political turmoil<br>处于政治动荡中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The country remained in political turmoil for nearly a decade.  这个国家在近十年里一直处于政治动荡之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uproar</td><td>/ˈʌˌprɔːr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>喧嚣，骚动</td><td>cause an uproar<br>引起轩然大波</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new tax plan caused an uproar among small business owners.  新税收方案在小企业主中引起轩然大波。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 whirl</td><td>/ˈwɜːrl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>急转，飞旋；旋转</td><td>whirl around<br>猛地转过身</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Snowflakes whirled madly in the beam of the streetlight.  雪花在路灯光柱中疯狂飞旋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 calm</td><td>/ˈkɑːm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、动词、名词</td><td>平静的；使镇静</td><td>calm down quickly<br>很快平静下来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke in a calm voice until the children settled.  她用平静的声音说话，直到孩子们安静下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 peace</td><td>/ˈpiːs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>和平，和睦；平静</td><td>live in peace<br>和睦相处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two villages have lived in peace for over a century.  这两个村庄和睦相处已逾一个世纪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tranquility</td><td>/træŋˈkwɪləti/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>宁静，淡定</td><td>an air of tranquility<br>宁静的气息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He enjoyed the tranquility of the snowy fields at dusk.  他享受黄昏时白雪覆盖的田野的静谧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>wheedle</b></td><td>/ˈwiːdl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>（用花言巧语）诱惑，哄骗</td><td>wheedle money out of him<br>哄他掏出钱来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She wheedled her father into lending her the old car.  她哄着父亲把那辆旧车借给她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 adulate</del></td><td>/ˈædʒəleɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>极度谄媚，拍马屁</td><td>adulate a celebrity<br>对名人极力吹捧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reporters adulated the young singer after her first hit.  记者们在这位年轻歌手第一首热门歌后对她大加吹捧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 blandish</del></td><td>/ˈblændɪʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>奉承，讨好，哄诱</td><td>blandish the boss<br>讨好奉承老板</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He blandished the officials until they signed the permit.  他一路奉承官员，直到他们签下许可。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cajole</td><td>/kəˈdʒoʊl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>哄骗</td><td>cajole him into agreeing<br>哄他答应下来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She cajoled her brother into driving her to the airport.  她哄着哥哥开车送她去机场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 coax</td><td>/koʊks/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>哄骗</td><td>coax him into agreeing<br>哄他答应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She coaxed the frightened kitten out from under the porch.  她把受惊的小猫从门廊下哄了出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 coerce</td><td>/koʊˈɜːrs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（以武力）强制</td><td>coerce him into silence<br>强迫他保持沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The soldiers coerced the villagers into handing over their grain.  士兵强迫村民交出粮食。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 compel</td><td>/kəmˈpel/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>强迫，迫使；使不得不</td><td>compel obedience<br>强制服从</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Illness compelled her to withdraw from the competition.  疾病迫使她退出了比赛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 demand</td><td>/dɪˈmænd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>强烈要求；需求</td><td>demand an apology<br>要求道歉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The angry customers demanded an apology from the store manager.  愤怒的顾客要求店长道歉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 force</td><td>/ˈfɔːrs/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>强迫，迫使；力量</td><td>force someone to confess<br>迫使某人认罪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The heavy rain forced them to cancel the outdoor ceremony.  大雨迫使他们取消了户外典礼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 oblige</td><td>/əˈblaɪdʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>帮忙，满足要求；使感激</td><td>happy to oblige<br>乐于帮忙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She asked for a second copy, and the clerk was glad to oblige.  她要了第二份，店员很乐意照办。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 require</td><td>/rɪˈkwaɪər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>要求，责令；需要</td><td>require him to apologize<br>责令他道歉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court required him to apologize to her in public.  法院责令他向她公开道歉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 8

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>whet</b></td><td>/wet/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>刺激，激起</td><td>whet the appetite<br>激起食欲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The smell of garlic whetted our appetite long before dinner.  蒜香早在开饭前就激起了我们的食欲。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arouse</td><td>/əˈraʊz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>唤起，激起；唤醒</td><td>arouse public suspicion<br>引起公众怀疑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The missing files aroused suspicion among the auditors.  丢失的文件引起了审计人员的怀疑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 bestir</del></td><td>/bɪˈstɜːr/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使振作，使行动起来</td><td>bestir himself to act<br>振作起来行动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He finally bestirred himself and began clearing the garage.  他终于振作起来，开始清理车库。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 elicit</td><td>/iˈlɪsɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激起，唤起</td><td>elicit a strong reaction<br>引起强烈反应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The king's speech elicited sustained cheers and applause from the crowd.  国王的演讲激起了人群持久的欢呼和掌声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 excite</td><td>/ɪkˈsaɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激起，引发；使兴奋</td><td>excite public interest<br>激起公众兴趣</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His speech excited strong resentment among the workers.  他的演讲激起了工人们的强烈不满。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 evoke</td><td>/ɪˈvoʊk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>唤起，引发</td><td>evoke childhood memories<br>唤起童年回忆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The smell of wet chalk evokes my primary school classroom.  湿粉笔的味道让我想起小学的教室。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ignite</td><td>/ɪɡˈnaɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>点燃，使燃烧；激起（感情）</td><td>ignite public outrage<br>激起公众愤怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The verdict ignited public outrage across the entire country within a few hours.  判决在几小时内就激起了全国的公众愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 kindle</td><td>/ˈkɪndl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>点燃</td><td>kindle a fire<br>点燃火堆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single spark can kindle a dangerous forest fire.  一颗火星就能点燃危险的森林大火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rally</td><td>/ˈræli/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>恢复，重振；集合；集会</td><td>rally after surgery<br>手术后恢复过来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The patient rallied surprisingly quickly after a week of treatment.  经过一周治疗，病人恢复得出人意料地快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stimulate</td><td>/ˈstɪmjəˌleɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>刺激，激发；促进</td><td>stimulate economic growth<br>促进经济增长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Warm rain stimulated the seeds to sprout within a week.  温暖的雨水刺激种子一周内发芽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 waken</td><td>/ˈweɪkən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>唤醒，醒来</td><td>waken the sleeping child<br>唤醒睡着的孩子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Try not to waken the baby when you come in.  你进来时尽量不要弄醒宝宝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 assuage</td><td>/əˈsweɪdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>缓和，减轻；平息</td><td>assuage his fears<br>减轻他的恐惧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor's speech did little to assuage public anger.  市长的演讲几乎没能平息公众的怒火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 allay</td><td>/əˈleɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>减轻，平息</td><td>allay public fears<br>平息公众恐慌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The statement was meant to allay concerns about safety.  这份声明意在减轻人们对安全的担忧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mitigate</td><td>/ˈmɪtɪɡeɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>减轻，使缓和</td><td>mitigate the damage<br>减轻损害</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Planting trees can mitigate the effects of summer heat.  种树可以减轻夏季高温的影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mollify</td><td>/ˈmɑːlɪfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>平息，抚慰，缓和</td><td>mollify an angry customer<br>安抚愤怒的顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sincere apology from the manager mollified the furious client.  经理诚挚的道歉安抚了这位愤怒的客户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pacify</td><td>/ˈpæsɪfaɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使平静，安慰</td><td>pacify a crying baby<br>安抚哭闹的婴儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nurse sang softly to pacify the frightened child before the injection.  打针前，护士轻声唱歌安抚受惊的孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 soothe</td><td>/suːð/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安慰，抚慰；减轻</td><td>soothe a crying baby<br>安抚哭闹的婴儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Soft music helped soothe the restless patient through the long night.  轻柔的音乐帮助安抚了焦躁的病人度过漫长的夜晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>winnow</b></td><td>/ˈwɪnoʊ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>筛选</td><td>winnow the applicants<br>筛选申请者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee will winnow the applicants down to five finalists.  委员会将把申请者筛选到只剩五名入围者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sift</td><td>/ˈsɪft/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>审查</td><td>sift through the evidence<br>细查证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investigators will sift through the evidence before naming any suspect.  调查人员会先细查证据，再点名任何嫌疑人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pick</td><td>/ˈpɪk/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>挑起（争斗）；挑选</td><td>pick a fight<br>挑起争斗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He picked a quarrel with everyone at the table.  他跟桌上每个人都挑起了争吵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 select</td><td>/səˈlekt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>精选的，上等的；挑选</td><td>a select group of guests<br>精选的一小群客人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a select few were invited to the private tasting.  只有精选的少数人受邀参加私人品鉴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 separate</td><td>/ˈsepəreɪt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>分开，分离</td><td>separate the yolks<br>把蛋黄分离出来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please separate the recyclable waste from the ordinary rubbish.  请把可回收垃圾与普通垃圾分开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>whimsical</b></td><td>/ˈwɪmzɪkl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>反复无常的</td><td>a whimsical decision<br>反复无常的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His whimsical changes of plan exhausted everyone on the small team.  他反复无常的计划变动让小团队里的每个人都疲惫不堪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 capricious</td><td>/kəˈprɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>反复无常的，善变的</td><td>capricious weather<br>变化无常的天气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The capricious wind kept shifting during the whole race.  整场比赛中变化无常的风一直在转向。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fickle</td><td>/ˈfɪkl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>易变的</td><td>fickle public taste<br>易变的大众品味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Public taste is fickle, so last year's hit sells poorly now.  大众品味易变，去年的热门如今销量很差。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 freakish</td><td>/ˈfriːkɪʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>反常的，怪异的</td><td>freakish weather<br>反常的天气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Freakish weather ruined the harvest across the whole valley.  反常的天气毁掉了整个山谷的收成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mercurial</td><td>/mɜːrˈkjʊriəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（情绪）善变的</td><td>a mercurial temper<br>善变的脾气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His mercurial temper made colleagues hesitate before bringing him any bad news.  他善变的脾气让同事在向他报告坏消息前都要犹豫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 volatile</td><td>/ˈvɑːlətl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>多变的</td><td>a volatile stock market<br>多变的股市</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investors hesitated because the stock market stayed volatile all spring.  由于股市整个春天都动荡多变，投资者犹豫不决。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 resolute</td><td>/ˈrezəluːt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚定的</td><td>remain resolute<br>保持坚定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Despite heavy criticism, the mayor remained resolute about closing the old factory.  尽管备受批评，市长在关闭旧工厂一事上依然坚定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unwavering</td><td>/ənˈweɪvərɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚定不移的，不动摇的</td><td>unwavering support<br>坚定不移的支持</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her unwavering belief in the plan convinced the whole committee.  她对方案坚定不移的信心说服了整个委员会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>wholesome</b></td><td>/ˈhoʊlsəm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有益身心健康的</td><td>wholesome home-cooked food<br>有益健康的家常饭菜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children grew strong on wholesome food and plenty of fresh air.  孩子们靠有益健康的食物和充足的新鲜空气长得很结实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 healthy</td><td>/ˈhelθi/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>健康的，有益健康的</td><td>a healthy diet<br>健康的饮食</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Walking daily is a healthy habit at any age.  每天散步在任何年龄都是健康习惯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 restorative</td><td>/rɪˈstɔːrətɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有益健康的</td><td>a restorative sleep<br>恢复元气的睡眠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A long walk by the sea proved wonderfully restorative after weeks of stress.  数周压力之后，在海边长时间散步格外有益身心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 salubrious</td><td>/səˈluːbriəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有益健康的</td><td>a salubrious mountain climate<br>有益健康的山地气候</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doctors sent him to a salubrious mountain village to recover his strength.  医生把他送到有益健康的山村去恢复体力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 recuperative</td><td>/rɪˈkuːpərətɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有助于恢复的，复原的</td><td>recuperative powers<br>恢复能力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mountain air had a wonderfully recuperative effect on her.  山间空气对她有极好的恢复作用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tonic</td><td>/ˈtɑːnɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>滋补的，有益健康的</td><td>a tonic effect<br>滋补的功效</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A week in the mountains had a tonic effect on her.  在山里待一周对她起了滋补强身的作用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 insalubrious</del></td><td>/ˌɪnsəˈlubriəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不利健康的，有害的</td><td>an insalubrious climate<br>不利健康的气候</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The damp, insalubrious basement made both children ill.  潮湿不卫生的地下室使两个孩子都病了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 noxious</td><td>/ˈnɑːkʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有害的，有毒的；产生强烈厌恶的</td><td>noxious fumes<br>有毒的烟气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Noxious fumes from the factory forced the school to close for two days.  工厂排出的有毒烟气迫使学校停课两天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unhealthy</td><td>/ʌnˈhelθi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不健康的，有害健康的</td><td>an unhealthy diet<br>不健康的饮食</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of an unhealthy diet finally caught up with him.  多年不健康的饮食终于让他付出了代价。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unwholesome</td><td>/ʌnˈhoʊlsəm/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有害的，不健康的</td><td>an unwholesome influence<br>不良的影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her parents thought the crowd an unwholesome influence.  她父母认为那群人是不良影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>wicked</b></td><td>/ˈwɪkɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>邪恶的</td><td>a wicked scheme<br>邪恶的计谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A wicked scheme to seize the estate was uncovered by the lawyer.  律师揭穿了一个夺取遗产的邪恶计谋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dark</td><td>/ˈdɑːrk/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>阴郁的；黑暗的</td><td>a dark mood<br>阴郁的心情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He fell into a dark mood after reading the letter.  读了那封信后他陷入阴郁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 evil</td><td>/ˈiːvəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>邪恶；邪恶的</td><td>the lesser evil<br>较小的祸害</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel examines how ordinary people come to commit evil.  这部小说探讨普通人如何走向作恶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 immoral</td><td>/ɪˈmɔːrəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不道德的</td><td>an immoral bargain<br>不道德的交易</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Many considered the deal immoral even if it was legal.  许多人认为这笔交易即使合法也不道德。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 iniquitous</td><td>/ɪˈnɪkwɪtəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>邪恶的，极不公正的</td><td>an iniquitous system<br>邪恶的制度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They fought against an iniquitous system of forced labor.  他们抗争一种邪恶的强迫劳动制度。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nefarious</td><td>/nɪˈferiəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极坏的，邪恶的</td><td>nefarious schemes<br>邪恶的计谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investigators uncovered nefarious schemes to divert relief money into private accounts.  调查人员揭露了把救灾款转入私人账户的邪恶计谋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sinful</td><td>/ˈsɪnfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有罪的，邪恶的</td><td>a sinful act<br>罪恶的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The preacher called greed the most sinful habit of our age.  牧师称贪婪是我们这个时代最罪恶的习性。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vicious</td><td>/ˈvɪʃəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>凶恶的，恶毒的</td><td>a vicious circle<br>恶性循环</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dog gave a vicious bite that required several stitches.  那只狗凶恶地咬了一口，需要缝几针。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 villainous</td><td>/ˈvɪlənəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>邪恶的，恶劣的</td><td>a villainous plot<br>邪恶的阴谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel turns on a villainous plot against the king.  这部小说围绕一个针对国王的邪恶阴谋展开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 decent</td><td>/ˈdiːsənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>得体的，正派的</td><td>decent and respectful behavior<br>得体而恭敬的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is a decent man who always keeps his promises.  他是个正派的人，总是守承诺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ethical</td><td>/ˈeθɪkəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>道德的，合乎道义的</td><td>ethical standards<br>道德标准</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reporters must weigh ethical limits before publishing private letters.  记者发表私人信件前必须权衡道德界限。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 good</td><td>/ˈɡʊd/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有利的，好的</td><td>a good opportunity<br>有利的机会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This is a good moment to renegotiate the contract.  这是重新谈判合同的有利时机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 honest</td><td>/ˈɑːnəst/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>诚实的，正直的</td><td>an honest answer<br>一个诚实的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Give me an honest answer, even if it disappoints me.  给我一个诚实的答复，即使会让我失望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 honorable</td><td>/ˈɑːnərəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>光荣的，可敬的</td><td>an honorable retreat<br>光荣的撤退</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She made the honorable choice and returned all the money.  她做出可敬的选择，把钱全退了回去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 moral</td><td>/ˈmɔːrəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>道德的；道德准则</td><td>a moral obligation<br>道义上的义务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They felt a moral obligation to shelter the refugees.  他们觉得有道义上的义务收留难民。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 righteous</td><td>/ˈraɪtʃəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>正直的，正义的</td><td>righteous indignation<br>义愤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her righteous indignation at the ruling filled the courtroom.  她对判决的义愤充满法庭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sublime</td><td>/səˈblaɪm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>崇高的；壮丽的；卓越的</td><td>a sublime landscape<br>崇高壮丽的景色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 From the summit we watched a sublime landscape unfold below us.  从山顶我们看着一片壮丽的景色在下方展开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 upright</td><td>/ˈʌpraɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>正直的，诚实的；竖直的</td><td>an upright citizen<br>一位正直的公民</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors described him as an upright and reliable man.  邻居们说他是个正直可靠的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 virtuous</td><td>/ˈvɜːrtʃuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>品德高尚的，正直的</td><td>a virtuous life<br>高尚的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He led a quiet, virtuous life and helped his neighbors freely.  他过着平静高尚的生活，乐于帮助邻里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>wince</b></td><td>/wɪns/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>畏缩</td><td>wince at the pain<br>因疼痛而龇牙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She winced as the nurse cleaned the deep cut on her arm.  护士清洗她手臂上的深口子时，她疼得直畏缩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blench</td><td>/blɛntʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>畏缩，退缩</td><td>blench at the sight<br>见状畏缩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He did not blench at the sight of the deep wound.  看到那道深伤口他也没有畏缩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cringe</td><td>/ˈkrɪndʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>卑躬屈膝；畏缩</td><td>cringe before the boss<br>在老板面前卑躬屈膝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The servant cringed whenever the duke raised his voice.  公爵一提高声音，仆人就畏缩起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quail</td><td>/kweɪl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>胆怯，畏缩</td><td>quail at the thought<br>一想到就发怵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even veteran soldiers quail at the thought of another winter campaign.  连老兵一想到又一场冬季战役也会畏缩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quiver</td><td>/ˈkwɪvər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>颤抖，微微发抖</td><td>quiver with fear<br>因恐惧而颤抖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her lip quivered but she refused to cry.  她的嘴唇颤抖，但她拒绝哭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 recoil</td><td>/rɪˈkɔɪl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>退却，畏缩</td><td>recoil in horror<br>惊恐地退缩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She recoiled in horror when the lamp revealed the broken door.  灯光照出被砸坏的门时，她惊恐地退缩了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shrink</td><td>/ʃrɪŋk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>（在数量或价值方面）降低，减小</td><td>shrink in value<br>价值缩水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The value of the old coins began to shrink after the scandal.  丑闻之后，那些旧币的价值开始缩水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tremble</td><td>/ˈtrembəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>颤抖，战栗</td><td>tremble with cold<br>冷得发抖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His hands trembled as he opened the sealed envelope.  他打开密封信封时双手发抖。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 confront</td><td>/kənˈfrʌnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>直接对抗，直面</td><td>confront the problem<br>直面问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sooner or later you must confront the problem directly.  你早晚必须直面这个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 face</td><td>/ˈfeɪs/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>面对，正视；脸，面</td><td>face the truth<br>正视事实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sooner or later she must face the truth about the debt.  她迟早得正视这笔债务的真相。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 meet</td><td>/miːt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>合适的</td><td>a meet response<br>合适的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A public apology seemed the only meet response to such a serious blunder.  公开道歉似乎是对如此严重失误唯一合适的回应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>windbag</b></td><td>/ˈwɪndbæɡ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>空话连篇的人，话痨</td><td>a pompous old windbag<br>自负的老话痨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The meeting dragged on because a pompous old windbag kept talking.  会议拖延不散，因为一个自负的老话痨一直在讲。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 babbler</td><td>/ˈbæblər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>喋喋不休的人</td><td>an endless babbler<br>没完没了的话痨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The talk show host was a cheerful babbler who never paused.  那位脱口秀主持人是个从不停口的快活话痨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conversationalist</td><td>/ˌkɑːnvərˈseɪʃənəlɪst/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>健谈者，谈话高手</td><td>a brilliant conversationalist<br>出色的健谈者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her uncle was a brilliant conversationalist at every dinner party.  她的叔叔在每次晚宴上都是出色的健谈者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 gabbler</del></td><td>/ˈɡæblər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>喋喋不休的人</td><td>a tireless gabbler<br>不知疲倦的话痨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nervous gabbler filled every silence with pointless chatter.  那个紧张的话痨用无意义的闲谈填满每段沉默。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 gasbag</del></td><td>/ˈɡæsbæɡ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>空话连篇的人</td><td>a political gasbag<br>满口空话的政客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Voters dismissed the candidate as a gasbag with no real plan.  选民认为这位候选人是个没有实际计划的空谈者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 prattler</del></td><td>/ˈprætlər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>唠叨不停的人</td><td>a cheerful prattler<br>快活的唠叨者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The child was a happy prattler from the moment she woke.  这孩子从醒来那刻起就唠叨不停。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>windy</b></td><td>/ˈwɪndi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冗长的</td><td>a windy speech<br>冗长空洞的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His windy speech said very little in more than an hour.  他冗长的演讲讲了一个多小时却几乎没什么内容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 circuitous</td><td>/sərˈkjuːɪtəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迂回的，不直接的</td><td>a circuitous route<br>迂回的路线</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We took a circuitous route to avoid the flooded highway.  我们绕了一条迂回的路以避开被淹的公路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 circumlocutory</del></td><td>/ˌsɜrkəmˈlɑkjətɔri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迂回冗赘的，绕弯子的</td><td>circumlocutory replies<br>绕弯子的答复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His circumlocutory answers hid a very simple refusal.  他绕弯子的答复掩盖了一个简单的拒绝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 diffuse</td><td>/dɪfˈjuːs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冗长散漫的；扩散的</td><td>a diffuse and wordy essay<br>散漫冗长的文章</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His argument was far too diffuse to convince a careful reader.  他的论证过于散漫，无法说服细心的读者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 garrulous</td><td>/ˈɡærələs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>啰嗦的，话多令人厌烦的</td><td>a garrulous neighbor<br>唠叨的邻居</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our garrulous neighbor talked for an hour about his lawn.  我们唠叨的邻居就他的草坪讲了一个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prolix</td><td>/ˈproʊlɪks/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>啰嗦的，冗长的</td><td>prolix legal prose<br>冗长的法律文字</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge criticized the prolix brief for burying its argument.  法官批评这份冗长的书状埋没了自己的论点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rambling</td><td>/ˈræmblɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>漫无目的的，散漫的</td><td>a rambling explanation<br>散漫的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave a rambling answer that satisfied nobody present.  他给出散漫的回答，在场无人满意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 verbose</td><td>/vɜːrˈboʊs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冗长的，啰嗦的</td><td>a verbose explanation<br>冗长的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her verbose report could have said the same thing in two pages.  她冗长的报告本可以用两页说完同样的内容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 compact</td><td>/ˈkɑːmpækt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>契约，协议；紧凑的</td><td>sign a compact<br>签订协议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two nations signed a compact to protect the shared river.  两国签订协议保护共有的河流。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 concise</td><td>/kənˈsaɪs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>简明的，简洁的</td><td>a concise explanation<br>简明的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Keep your answer concise; the interviewer has only ten minutes.  回答要简洁，面试官只有十分钟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 crisp</td><td>/ˈkrɪsp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>挺括整洁的；脆的</td><td>a crisp white shirt<br>挺括的白衬衫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wore a crisp white shirt to the interview.  他穿着一件挺括的白衬衫去面试。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pithy</td><td>/ˈpɪθi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>简练的，言简意赅的</td><td>a pithy remark<br>精练的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her pithy summary captured the whole debate in two sentences.  她简练的总结用两句话概括了整场辩论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 succinct</td><td>/səkˈsɪŋkt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>简明的，简洁的</td><td>a succinct summary<br>简洁的总结</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her succinct summary covered the entire report in three short paragraphs.  她简洁的总结用三小段概括了整份报告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 terse</td><td>/tɜːrs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>简洁的，简明的</td><td>a terse reply<br>简洁的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His terse note said only that the deal had collapsed.  他简短的字条只说交易破裂了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>winsome</b></td><td>/ˈwɪnsəm/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，漂亮的</td><td>a winsome smile<br>迷人的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her winsome smile made the shy children relax almost at once.  她迷人的微笑让害羞的孩子们几乎立刻放松下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adorable</td><td>/əˈdɔːrəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可爱的，讨人喜欢的</td><td>an adorable puppy<br>可爱的小狗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their adorable puppy won over everyone in the office.  他们可爱的小狗赢得了办公室所有人的喜爱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 charming</td><td>/ˈtʃɑːrmɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，可爱的</td><td>a charming little village<br>迷人的小村庄</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We spent the afternoon in a charming village by the lake.  我们在湖边一个迷人的村庄度过了下午。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disarming</td><td>/dɪˈsɑːrmɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>消除敌意的，讨喜的</td><td>a disarming smile<br>令人释怀的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His disarming honesty won over even the harshest critics.  他令人释然的坦诚甚至赢得了最严厉批评者的好感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enchanting</td><td>/ɪnˈtʃæntɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，醉人的</td><td>an enchanting melody<br>醉人的旋律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The garden looked enchanting under the soft light of lanterns.  花园在灯笼柔和的光线下显得十分迷人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 endearing</td><td>/ɪnˈdɪrɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人喜爱的</td><td>an endearing habit<br>讨人喜欢的习惯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The puppy's clumsy walk was oddly endearing to everyone.  小狗笨拙的走姿让所有人莫名喜爱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sweet</td><td>/ˈswiːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>甜的；温和的，亲切的</td><td>in a sweet tone<br>以温和的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke in a sweet voice that calmed the frightened child.  她用温柔的声音说话，安抚了害怕的孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 winning</td><td>/ˈwɪnɪŋ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，动人的</td><td>a winning smile<br>迷人的笑容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She greeted every guest with a winning smile.  她用迷人的笑容迎接每位客人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 abhorrent</td><td>/æbˈhɔːrənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的</td><td>abhorrent to most people<br>令多数人厌恶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The practice is abhorrent to anyone with basic decency.  这种做法令任何有基本良知的人厌恶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 abominable</td><td>/əˈbɑːmənəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可憎的，极糟的</td><td>abominable behavior<br>可憎的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His abominable behavior at dinner shocked the guests.  他晚餐时可憎的举止让客人震惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 detestable</td><td>/dɪˈtɛstəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可憎的，令人厌恶的</td><td>a detestable habit<br>令人厌恶的习惯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He found the general's casual cruelty absolutely detestable.  他觉得那位将军随意的残暴极其可憎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hateful</td><td>/ˈheɪtfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可憎的，令人厌恶的</td><td>a hateful remark<br>可憎的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He apologized for the hateful remark he had posted online.  他为自己在网上发的可憎言论道歉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 loathsome</td><td>/ˈloʊðsəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的</td><td>a loathsome smell<br>令人厌恶的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A loathsome smell rose from the neglected drain.  无人清理的下水道升起令人厌恶的气味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 odious</td><td>/ˈoʊdiəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可憎的，令人厌恶的</td><td>an odious task<br>讨厌的差事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Comparing the two writers seems both odious and pointless.  比较这两位作家显得可憎又无意义。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>wit</b></td><td>/wɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>机智，智慧</td><td>a flash of wit<br>一闪的机智</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her quick wit saved the interview after the microphone failed.  麦克风出故障后，是她的机敏救了那场访谈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 astuteness</td><td>/əˈstuːtnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>精明，敏锐</td><td>political astuteness<br>政治上的精明</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her astuteness in negotiations saved the company millions.  她在谈判中的精明为公司节省了数百万。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brilliance</td><td>/ˈbrɪljəns/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>才华出众；光辉灿烂</td><td>the brilliance of his argument<br>他论证的精彩之处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics praised the brilliance of the young director's first film.  批评家盛赞这位年轻导演处女作的精彩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 foxiness</del></td><td>/ˈfɑːksinəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>狡猾，机敏</td><td>sheer foxiness<br>十足的狡猾</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old trader's foxiness kept him ahead of his rivals.  这位老商人的狡猾使他始终领先对手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intelligence</td><td>/ɪnˈtelɪdʒəns/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>智力，才智；情报</td><td>keen intelligence<br>敏锐的才智</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her keen intelligence showed in every answer she gave.  她敏锐的才智体现在她的每个回答中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 keenness</td><td>/ˈkiːnnəs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>敏锐，锐利；热切</td><td>keenness of perception<br>敏锐的洞察力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old judge was admired for the keenness of his legal mind.  这位老法官因其敏锐的法律头脑而受人敬佩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perspicacity</td><td>/ˌpɜːrspɪˈkæsɪti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>洞察力，聪颖</td><td>remarkable perspicacity<br>非凡的洞察力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The critic was praised for the perspicacity of her essays.  这位评论家因文章的洞察力受到赞扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sagacity</td><td>/səˈɡæsɪti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>睿智，聪慧</td><td>political sagacity<br>政治上的睿智</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old judge was known for his sagacity and calm.  这位老法官以睿智和沉稳闻名。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sharpness</td><td>/ˈʃɑːrpnəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>敏锐，锐利</td><td>sharpness of mind<br>思维的敏锐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Age had not dulled the sharpness of his mind.  年龄并未使他思维的敏锐减退。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shrewdness</td><td>/ˈʃruːdnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>精明，敏锐的判断力</td><td>political shrewdness<br>政治上的精明</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took real shrewdness to buy the land before prices rose.  在地价上涨之前买下那块地需要真正的精明。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 illuminati</td><td>/ɪˌluːmɪˈnɑːti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>智者</td><td>the illuminati of the age<br>当代的智者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The salon gathered the illuminati of eighteenth-century Paris.  这个沙龙聚集了十八世纪巴黎的智者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pundit</td><td>/ˈpʌndɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>权威人士，专家</td><td>a television pundit<br>电视上的评论专家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Every pundit predicted a close race and every one was wrong.  每位评论专家都预测选情接近，结果都错了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sage</td><td>/seɪdʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>智者</td><td>an ancient sage<br>古代的智者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Villagers walked for days to ask the old sage for advice.  村民们走了好几天去向那位老智者求教。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 savant</td><td>/sæˈvɑːnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>博学之士，学者</td><td>a quiet savant<br>沉静的博学之士</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The quiet savant spent forty years cataloguing insects in a small museum.  那位沉静的博学之士在小博物馆里花了四十年为昆虫编目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scholar</td><td>/ˈskɑːlər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>学者，博学之人</td><td>a distinguished scholar<br>杰出的学者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lecture drew scholars from four different countries.  这场讲座吸引了四个国家的学者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 brainlessness</del></td><td>/ˈbreɪnləsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>愚蠢，没头脑</td><td>sheer brainlessness<br>十足的愚蠢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The brainlessness of the plan was obvious to everyone present.  这个计划的愚蠢在场所有人都看得出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dullness</td><td>/ˈdʌlnəs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>乏味，单调；迟钝</td><td>the dullness of the lecture<br>讲座的乏味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dullness of the report hid several important findings.  这份报告的乏味掩盖了几项重要发现。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 fatuity</del></td><td>/fəˈtuːɪti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>愚昧；蠢话</td><td>the fatuity of the remark<br>那句话的愚蠢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics mocked the fatuity of the government's latest promises.  批评者嘲笑政府最新承诺的愚昧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lunacy</td><td>/ˈluːnəsi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>极其愚蠢的行为；精神失常</td><td>sheer lunacy<br>十足的荒唐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Driving through that storm would have been sheer lunacy.  在那场暴风雨中开车纯属荒唐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 silliness</td><td>/ˈsɪlinəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>愚蠢，无聊可笑</td><td>childish silliness<br>孩子气的傻闹</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher tolerated a little silliness at the end of class.  老师容许下课前有点傻闹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 9

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>withdraw</b></td><td>/wɪðˈdrɔː/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>取出（款项）；撤回，撤退</td><td>withdraw money from the bank<br>从银行取钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stopped at the bank to withdraw money before the long trip.  他在长途旅行前去银行取钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 recede</td><td>/rɪˈsiːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>退去，消退，减弱</td><td>recede into the distance<br>渐渐远去消失</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The floodwaters slowly receded from the village streets.  洪水缓缓从村庄的街道退去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 retreat</td><td>/riˈtriːt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>避难所，隐居处</td><td>a mountain retreat<br>山间隐居处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cabin became his retreat from the noise of the city.  那间小屋成了他躲避城市喧闹的避难所。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fall back</td><td>/ˌfɔːl ˈbæk/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>撤退，后退；求助于</td><td>fall back to safer ground<br>退到更安全的位置</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The troops had to fall back when their ammunition ran out.  弹药耗尽时部队不得不撤退。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 advance</td><td>/ədˈvæns/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>前进；促进；预付款</td><td>advance a theory<br>提出一种理论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The army advanced slowly through the thick forest at dawn.  黎明时军队缓慢穿过密林前进。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>wither</b></td><td>/ˈwɪðər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>枯萎，凋谢</td><td>wither in the heat<br>在高温中枯萎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tomato plants withered after a week without rain or shade.  一周无雨又没有遮荫，番茄苗都枯萎了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dry</td><td>/ˈdraɪ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>干的，干燥的；使干燥</td><td>bone dry soil<br>干透的土壤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Hang the towels outside until they are completely dry.  把毛巾挂到外面直到完全干燥。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fade</td><td>/ˈfeɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>衰退，消退；褪色</td><td>hopes began to fade<br>希望开始消退</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their hopes of rescue began to fade after three days.  三天后他们获救的希望开始消退。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shrivel</td><td>/ˈʃrɪvəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>皱缩，枯萎，干瘪</td><td>shrivel in the sun<br>在阳光下皱缩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unwatered seedlings shriveled within two days of intense heat.  没浇水的幼苗在两天酷热中就干瘪了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wane</td><td>/weɪn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>减少，衰退</td><td>wax and wane<br>盈亏消长，兴衰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Public interest in the scandal waned within a month.  公众对这桩丑闻的兴趣一个月内就减退了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wilt</td><td>/wɪlt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>精神萎靡，憔悴</td><td>wilt in the heat<br>在酷热中萎靡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The runners began to wilt in the heat of the final mile.  跑到最后一英里的酷热中，选手们开始萎靡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 wizen</del></td><td>/ˈwɪzən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使枯萎；使干瘪</td><td>wizen with age<br>因年老而干瘪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The apples wizened in the cellar over the long winter.  苹果在地窖里经过漫长的冬天变得干瘪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 revive</td><td>/rɪˈvaɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>再获新生</td><td>revive after the crisis<br>危机后重获生机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Local trade began to revive once the new highway opened last spring.  去年春天新高速开通后，当地贸易开始重获生机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>withhold</b></td><td>/wɪðˈhoʊld/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抑制；扣压，不给予</td><td>withhold information<br>隐瞒信息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was accused of withholding key information.  他被指控隐瞒关键信息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 decline</td><td>/dɪˈklaɪn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>拒绝；下降，衰退</td><td>decline an invitation<br>谢绝邀请</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She politely declined the invitation to speak at the ceremony.  她礼貌地谢绝了在典礼上发言的邀请。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disallow</td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈlaʊ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>不许，驳回</td><td>disallow the goal<br>判进球无效</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The referee disallowed the goal for an obvious offside.  裁判因明显越位判该进球无效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disapprove</td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈpruːv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>不赞成，反对</td><td>disapprove of the plan<br>不赞成该计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her parents strongly disapprove of the plan to drop out.  她父母强烈不赞成退学的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 detain</td><td>/dɪˈteɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>拘留，扣押；留住</td><td>detain a suspect<br>拘留嫌疑人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police detained the two men for questioning about the missing car.  警方拘留了这两名男子，就失踪汽车一事问话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 refuse</td><td>/rɪˈfjuːz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>拒绝，不肯；废弃物</td><td>refuse to cooperate<br>拒绝合作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness refused to answer any further questions in court.  证人拒绝在法庭上回答任何进一步的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reject</td><td>/rɪˈdʒekt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>拒绝，驳回；废品</td><td>reject the proposal<br>否决提案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee voted to reject the proposal without further discussion.  委员会投票否决了该提案，不再讨论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reprobate</td><td>/ˈreprəbeɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>堕落者，道德败坏的人</td><td>an old reprobate<br>一个老无赖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel's hero is a charming reprobate with no money.  小说的主角是个迷人却身无分文的堕落者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 restrain</td><td>/rɪˈstreɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抑制，约束，制止</td><td>restrain his temper<br>克制他的脾气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She could hardly restrain her laughter during the speech.  演讲时她几乎忍不住笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 retain</td><td>/rɪˈteɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>保留，留住</td><td>retain the original title<br>保留原名</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The museum retained the building's original facade during the renovation.  翻修时博物馆保留了建筑的原有正面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 allow</td><td>/əˈlaʊ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>允许，准许</td><td>allow visitors inside<br>允许访客进入</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The library allows visitors to photograph the manuscripts without flash.  图书馆允许访客不开闪光灯拍摄手稿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 concede</td><td>/kənˈsiːd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>承认</td><td>concede the point<br>承认这一点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She finally conceded that her opponent had made a fair point.  她最终承认对手说得有道理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 grant</td><td>/ˈɡrænt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>承认；授予，同意给予</td><td>grant that he is right<br>承认他是对的</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I grant that the plan has some obvious weaknesses.  我承认这个计划有些明显的弱点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 permit</td><td>/pərˈmɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>允许，准许；许可证</td><td>permit access<br>允许进入</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The school does not permit phones during classroom hours.  学校不允许上课时间使用手机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>wizen</b></del></td><td>/ˈwɪzən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使枯萎；使干瘪</td><td>wizen with age<br>因年老而干瘪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The apples wizened in the cellar over the long winter.  苹果在地窖里经过漫长的冬天变得干瘪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dry</td><td>/ˈdraɪ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>干的，干燥的；使干燥</td><td>bone dry soil<br>干透的土壤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Hang the towels outside until they are completely dry.  把毛巾挂到外面直到完全干燥。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 mummify</del></td><td>/ˈmʌməfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使成木乃伊；使干枯</td><td>mummify a body<br>把遗体制成木乃伊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Priests mummified the king's body before the long burial rites.  祭司在漫长的葬礼前将国王遗体制成木乃伊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shrivel</td><td>/ˈʃrɪvəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>皱缩，枯萎，干瘪</td><td>shrivel in the sun<br>在阳光下皱缩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unwatered seedlings shriveled within two days of intense heat.  没浇水的幼苗在两天酷热中就干瘪了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wither</td><td>/ˈwɪðər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>枯萎，凋谢</td><td>wither in the heat<br>在高温中枯萎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tomato plants withered after a week without rain or shade.  一周无雨又没有遮荫，番茄苗都枯萎了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 faded</td><td>/ˈfeɪdəd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>褪色的；枯萎的</td><td>faded curtains<br>褪色的窗帘</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The faded curtains had hung in that window for thirty years.  那些褪色的窗帘在那扇窗上挂了三十年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 withered</td><td>/ˈwɪðərd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>枯萎的，干瘪的</td><td>withered leaves<br>枯萎的叶子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Withered leaves crunched under our boots along the path.  枯叶在我们靴下的小路上嘎吱作响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 revive</td><td>/rɪˈvaɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>再获新生</td><td>revive after the crisis<br>危机后重获生机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Local trade began to revive once the new highway opened last spring.  去年春天新高速开通后，当地贸易开始重获生机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>wobble</b></td><td>/ˈwɑːbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>摇晃，颤抖；犹豫不决</td><td>wobble on the ice<br>在冰上摇晃</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old table wobbles whenever someone leans on its left side.  只要有人靠在左边，这张旧桌子就会摇晃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rock</td><td>/ˈrɑːk/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使震惊，动摇；摇动</td><td>rock the nation<br>震动全国</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scandal rocked the party just before the election.  这桩丑闻在选举前震动了该党。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 totter</td><td>/ˈtɑːtər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>摇摇晃晃地走，蹒跚</td><td>totter across the room<br>摇晃着走过房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The toddler tottered a few steps and then sat down.  幼儿摇摇晃晃走了几步就坐下了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 falter</td><td>/ˈfɔːltər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>蹒跚；犹豫，踌躇</td><td>falter in his resolve<br>决心动摇</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Mr. Garrison never faltered in his resolve to finish the project.  加里森先生完成这个项目的决心从未动摇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stagger</td><td>/ˈstæɡər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>摇晃地走，蹒跚</td><td>stagger to his feet<br>摇摇晃晃站起来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The exhausted runner staggered across the finish line.  疲惫的跑者摇摇晃晃冲过终点线。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 teeter</td><td>/ˈtiːtər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>摇晃，摇摇欲坠</td><td>teeter on the edge<br>在边缘摇摇欲坠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company teetered on the edge of bankruptcy for several months.  公司数月来一直在破产边缘摇摇欲坠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vacillate</td><td>/ˈvæsəleɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>犹豫不决；摇动，摇摆</td><td>vacillate between two options<br>在两个选择间犹豫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Parents who vacillate between approval and refusal confuse their children.  在同意与反对之间摇摆不定的父母会让孩子困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 waver</td><td>/ˈweɪvər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>动摇，犹豫不决</td><td>waver between two options<br>在两个选项间摇摆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wavered for weeks before accepting the job offer.  他犹豫了好几周才接受这份工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stabilize</td><td>/ˈsteɪbəˌlaɪz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使稳定，使平稳</td><td>stabilize the ladder<br>把梯子稳住</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two ropes stabilized the ladder against the shaking wall.  两根绳子把梯子在晃动的墙上稳住了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>worldly</b></td><td>/ˈwɜːrldli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>世间的，世俗的；老练的</td><td>worldly possessions<br>世俗财产</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave away all his worldly possessions and entered the monastery.  他捐出全部世俗财产，进了修道院。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 carnal</td><td>/ˈkɑːrnl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>肉体的，物质的；世俗的</td><td>carnal pleasures<br>肉体的享乐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The monk warned his students against carnal pleasures and idle talk.  僧人告诫学生远离肉体享乐和闲谈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 corporeal</td><td>/kɔːrˈpɔːriəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>肉体的；有形的，实体的</td><td>corporeal existence<br>肉体的存在</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ghost seemed almost corporeal in the flickering candlelight.  在摇曳的烛光中，那鬼影几乎像有实体。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 material</td><td>/məˈtɪriəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>物质的；重要的；材料</td><td>material comforts<br>物质上的享受</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He cared little for material wealth and lived very simply.  他不太在意物质财富，生活极为简朴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mundane</td><td>/mʌnˈdeɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>平凡的</td><td>mundane daily chores<br>平凡的日常杂务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent the morning on mundane daily chores like laundry and dishes.  他整个上午都在做洗衣洗碗这类平凡的日常杂务。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cosmopolitan</td><td>/ˌkɑːzməˈpɑːlɪtən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>有世界性眼光的，包容的</td><td>a cosmopolitan city<br>国际化的都市</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years abroad gave her a cosmopolitan outlook her old classmates clearly lacked.  多年国外生活给了她老同学明显缺乏的世界性眼光。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sophisticated</td><td>/səˈfɪstɪkeɪtɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>复杂精密的；老练的</td><td>a sophisticated system<br>复杂精密的系统</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lab uses highly sophisticated equipment to detect the particles.  实验室用极为精密的设备探测这些粒子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mental</td><td>/ˈmentəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>精神的，心理的</td><td>mental health<br>心理健康</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Long hours took a toll on his mental health.  长时间工作损害了他的心理健康。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 spiritual</td><td>/ˈspɪrɪtʃuəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>精神的；灵魂的，宗教的</td><td>spiritual growth<br>精神成长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She turned to meditation in search of spiritual peace.  她转向冥想，以寻求精神上的平静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ingenuous</td><td>/ɪnˈdʒenjuəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>天真淳朴的</td><td>an ingenuous young student<br>天真淳朴的年轻学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His ingenuous questions about the city amused the veteran taxi driver greatly.  他关于这座城市的天真提问让老出租车司机大为好笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 innocent</td><td>/ˈɪnəsənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的，无恶意的；无辜的</td><td>an innocent remark<br>无恶意的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 What began as an innocent joke ended in a serious quarrel.  一句本无恶意的玩笑最后引发了激烈争吵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 naive</td><td>/naɪˈiːv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>天真的，幼稚的</td><td>naive assumptions<br>天真的假设</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was naive to expect honesty from someone with that record.  指望有那种记录的人诚实太天真了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unsophisticated</td><td>/ˌʌnsəˈfɪstɪkeɪtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单纯的，不老练的</td><td>an unsophisticated buyer<br>不老练的买家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Unsophisticated investors are often the first to lose their money.  不老练的投资者往往最先赔钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>windfall</b></td><td>/ˈwɪndfɔːl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>意外之财；意外的好运</td><td>an unexpected windfall<br>意外之财</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unexpected windfall allowed them to repay the whole loan early.  这笔意外之财让他们提前还清了全部贷款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 benediction</td><td>/ˌbenəˈdɪkʃən/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恩赐，福音；祝祷</td><td>a benediction for farmers<br>对农民的恩赐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rain after the long drought was a benediction for local farmers.  久旱之后的这场雨是当地农民的恩赐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blessing</td><td>/ˈblesɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>幸事，福气</td><td>a mixed blessing<br>好坏兼有的事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The early rain was a blessing for the dry fields.  早来的雨对干旱的田地是一件幸事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 boon</td><td>/buːn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恩惠，福利</td><td>a boon to farmers<br>对农民的福音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This solar charger is a real boon to travelling photographers.  这款太阳能充电器对旅行摄影师来说是一大福音。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 felicity</td><td>/fɪˈlɪsəti/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>得体，恰当；幸福</td><td>felicity of expression<br>措辞得体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her essays are admired for their felicity of expression.  她的散文以措辞得体而受赞赏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 godsend</td><td>/ˈɡɑːdˌsend/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>天赐之物，及时的帮助</td><td>prove a godsend<br>成为天赐的帮助</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The extra grant was a godsend to the small clinic.  这笔额外拨款对小诊所是天赐之助。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 manna</td><td>/ˈmænə/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>意外的恩赐</td><td>manna from heaven<br>天降的恩赐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The donation felt like manna to the struggling school.  这笔捐款对困难的学校像是天降恩赐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 apocalypse</td><td>/əˈpɑːkəˌlɪps/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>大灾难；世界末日</td><td>a nuclear apocalypse<br>核末日灾难</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel imagines an apocalypse caused by a runaway climate crisis.  这部小说设想了失控气候危机引发的末日灾难。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 calamity</td><td>/kəˈlæməti/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>灾难，大祸；不幸事件</td><td>a national calamity<br>国家性的大灾难</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The flood was the worst calamity the small town had faced.  这场洪水是这个小镇遭遇过的最严重灾难。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cataclysm</td><td>/ˈkætəklɪzəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>大灾难；剧变</td><td>a social cataclysm<br>社会剧变</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The eruption was a cataclysm that reshaped the entire island.  那次喷发是一场重塑整座岛屿的大灾难。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 catastrophe</td><td>/kəˈtæstrəfi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>大灾难；彻底的失败</td><td>an ecological catastrophe<br>生态大灾难</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The oil spill became an ecological catastrophe for the whole coast.  漏油事件成为整个海岸的生态大灾难。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 debacle</td><td>/deɪˈbɑːkl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>溃败</td><td>an economic debacle<br>经济上的溃败</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The campaign ended in a debacle that cost the party three seats.  这场竞选以溃败收场，使该党丢掉三个席位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 scourge</td><td>/skɜːrdʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>祸患，灾难；鞭子</td><td>the scourge of famine<br>饥荒之祸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Famine was the scourge of the region for a whole decade.  饥荒在整整十年间是该地区的祸患。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>wrangle</b></td><td>/ˈræŋɡl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>纷争，争端</td><td>a legal wrangle<br>一场法律纷争</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A long legal wrangle over the land delayed construction for years.  一场关于这块土地的漫长法律纷争使工程推迟数年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 altercation</td><td>/ˌɑːltərˈkeɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>激烈争吵，口角</td><td>a heated altercation<br>一场激烈的争吵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A brief altercation over parking escalated into a lawsuit.  一场关于停车的短暂争吵最后升级成了诉讼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 controversy</td><td>/ˈkɑːntrəˌvɜːrsi/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>争论，争议</td><td>spark a controversy<br>引发一场争议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painting sparked a controversy that lasted for decades.  这幅画引发了持续数十年的争议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disagreement</td><td>/dɪsəˈɡriːmənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>分歧，不一致，争执</td><td>a sharp disagreement<br>激烈的分歧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A long disagreement over funding stalled the whole project.  关于资金的长期分歧使整个项目停滞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dispute</td><td>/dɪˈspjuːt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>争论，争端；对…提出质疑</td><td>settle a dispute<br>解决争端</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The neighbors are still in dispute over the fence.  邻居们仍在为那道围栏争执。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fight</td><td>/ˈfaɪt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>对抗，斗争；打斗</td><td>fight the proposal<br>反对这项提案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The residents will fight the proposal to widen the old highway.  居民们将反对拓宽这条旧公路的提案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imbroglio</td><td>/ɪmˈbroʊlioʊ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>困境，复杂的局面</td><td>a political imbroglio<br>政治上的乱局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister resigned in the middle of a diplomatic imbroglio.  部长在一场外交乱局之中辞职了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quarrel</td><td>/ˈkwɔːrəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>争吵，口角</td><td>pick a quarrel<br>找人吵架</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The brothers had a bitter quarrel about their father's will.  兄弟俩为父亲的遗嘱激烈争吵了一场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 squabble</td><td>/ˈskwɑːbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>为琐事争吵；口角</td><td>squabble over money<br>为钱争吵不休</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children squabble endlessly about who sits by the window.  孩子们为谁坐窗边没完没了地吵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 altercate</del></td><td>/ˈɔltərkeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>争吵，激烈争辩</td><td>altercate over money<br>为钱争吵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The brothers altercated over the will for the entire afternoon.  兄弟俩为遗嘱争吵了整个下午。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 argue</td><td>/ˈɑːrɡju/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>争论，争吵；主张</td><td>argue over money<br>为钱争吵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They argued over money for most of the evening.  他们为钱争吵了大半个晚上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 er</td><td>/ər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>呃（犹豫时的语气声）</td><td>a hesitant er<br>犹豫的一声「呃」</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered with a hesitant er and then fell silent.  他犹豫地「呃」了一声，然后沉默了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 controvert</del></td><td>/ˈkɑntrəvɜrt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>反驳，否证</td><td>controvert the claim<br>反驳该说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New fossil data controvert the long-accepted theory of migration.  新的化石数据反驳了长期公认的迁徙理论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hassle</td><td>/ˈhæsəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>争吵；麻烦事</td><td>a hassle over money<br>为钱的一场争吵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They had a long hassle over who should pay.  他们为该谁付钱争吵了很久。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quibble</td><td>/ˈkwɪbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>吹毛求疵</td><td>quibble over details<br>在细节上挑刺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They quibbled over the wording while the deadline slipped away.  他们在措辞上纠缠不休，截止日期却悄悄过去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spat</td><td>/spæt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>（小的）争吵</td><td>a spat over money<br>为钱的小争吵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The couple had a brief spat over money and then apologized.  这对夫妻为钱小吵一场，随后互相道歉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tiff</td><td>/tɪf/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>小争吵</td><td>have a tiff with him<br>和他闹小别扭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had a silly tiff with him about the dinner plans.  她和他为晚餐安排闹了一场无谓的小别扭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 harmony</td><td>/ˈhɑːrməni/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>和谐，融洽</td><td>live in harmony with<br>与…和谐共处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two communities have lived in harmony for generations.  这两个社区世代和睦相处。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>wretched</b></td><td>/ˈretʃɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极差的；沮丧的</td><td>wretched living conditions<br>恶劣的居住条件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Families lived in wretched conditions beside the flooded riverbank.  一些家庭在泛滥的河岸边过着极差的生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bad</td><td>/ˈbæd/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶意的；坏的，糟的</td><td>bad intentions<br>不良的居心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was no bad feeling between the two old rivals.  两位老对手之间并没有恶意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 coarse</td><td>/kɔːrs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗糙的，表面不平整的；粗俗的</td><td>coarse language<br>粗俗的言语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The workers used coarse language that shocked the visiting students.  工人们说的粗话让来访的学生们大吃一惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inferior</td><td>/ɪnˈfɪriər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>低等的，次等的</td><td>inferior in quality<br>质量较差</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Customers complained that the new fabric was clearly inferior.  顾客抱怨新面料明显质量较差。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 low-grade</td><td>/ˌloʊ ˈɡreɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>低品质的，低级的</td><td>low-grade materials<br>劣质材料</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The builder used low-grade materials in order to cut costs.  建筑商为压缩成本使用了劣质材料。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mediocre</td><td>/ˌmiːdiˈoʊkər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>平庸的；质量中等偏下的</td><td>a mediocre performance<br>平庸的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The orchestra's mediocre performance disappointed listeners who had waited months for the concert.  乐团平庸的表演让等了几个月的听众很失望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 miserable</td><td>/ˈmɪzərəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>痛苦的，凄惨的</td><td>feel utterly miserable<br>感到极其痛苦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cold rain made the whole camping trip miserable.  冰冷的雨让整个露营之行变得凄惨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 poor</td><td>/pʊr/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫瘠的，贫乏的；贫穷的</td><td>poor rocky soil<br>贫瘠的石质土壤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The harvest was poor after a whole summer without rain.  整个夏天没下雨，收成很差。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 rubbishy</del></td><td>/ˈrʌbɪʃi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>垃圾般的，毫无价值的</td><td>rubbishy novels<br>毫无价值的小说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wasted the whole summer reading rubbishy detective novels.  他浪费了整个夏天读毫无价值的侦探小说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 terrible</td><td>/ˈterəbəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的；糟糕的</td><td>a terrible storm<br>可怕的风暴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They lost everything in a terrible fire last winter.  去年冬天一场可怕的火灾让他们失去一切。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trashy</td><td>/ˈtræʃi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>低劣的，无价值的</td><td>trashy magazines<br>低劣的杂志</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She admitted enjoying trashy magazines on long flights.  她承认在长途航班上喜欢看低劣杂志。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blue</td><td>/ˈbluː/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>下流的，猥亵的；蓝色的</td><td>blue humor<br>低俗的黄色幽默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His routine was too blue for a family audience.  他的表演对家庭观众来说太下流了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crestfallen</td><td>/ˈkrestfɔːlən/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>垂头丧气的，沮丧的</td><td>look utterly crestfallen<br>显得十分沮丧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He came home crestfallen after losing the final match by one point.  决赛以一分之差落败后，他垂头丧气地回了家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dejected</td><td>/dɪˈdʒektɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>沮丧的，失望的</td><td>a dejected look<br>沮丧的神情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She left the interview dejected and sure she had failed.  她面试后神情沮丧，确信自己失败了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 doleful</td><td>/ˈdoʊlfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>悲哀的，忧郁的</td><td>a doleful voice<br>悲哀的语调</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She told the whole story in a low doleful voice.  她用低沉悲哀的语调讲完了整件事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dolorous</td><td>/ˈdoʊlərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>忧伤的</td><td>a dolorous sigh<br>忧伤的叹息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A dolorous sigh escaped him whenever the subject of his hometown arose.  每次提到故乡，他都发出忧伤的叹息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gloomy</td><td>/ˈɡluːmi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>忧郁的</td><td>a gloomy mood<br>忧郁的心情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She grew gloomy whenever the winter rain lasted more than a week.  每当冬雨连下一周以上，她就变得忧郁起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 melancholy</td><td>/ˈmelənkɑːli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>忧郁，伤感</td><td>a mood of quiet melancholy<br>淡淡的忧郁情绪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Autumn rain always fills him with a vague melancholy.  秋雨总让他心里泛起一种莫名的忧郁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mournful</td><td>/ˈmɔːrnfl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>悲伤的</td><td>a mournful melody<br>悲伤的旋律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A mournful melody drifted from the church as the procession passed by.  送葬队伍经过时，教堂里飘出一段悲伤的旋律。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rueful</td><td>/ˈruːfəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>悔恨的，懊悔的</td><td>a rueful smile<br>懊悔的苦笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He admitted his error with a rueful smile and a shrug.  他带着懊悔的苦笑和一个耸肩承认了错误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sorrowful</td><td>/ˈsɑːroʊfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>悲伤的，忧伤的</td><td>a sorrowful farewell<br>悲伤的告别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her sorrowful eyes told us the news before she spoke.  她悲伤的眼睛在她开口前就告诉了我们消息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 woeful</td><td>/ˈwoʊfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>悲惨的，糟糕的</td><td>a woeful performance<br>糟糕的表现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team's woeful defending cost them the championship again.  球队糟糕的防守又一次让他们丢了冠军。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 excellent</td><td>/ˈeksələnt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>优秀的，极好的</td><td>excellent results<br>优异的成绩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The students achieved excellent results in the final exam.  学生们在期末考试中取得了优异成绩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fine</td><td>/ˈfaɪn/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>细微的，精细的</td><td>a fine distinction<br>细微的差别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He drew a fine distinction between courage and recklessness.  他在勇敢与莽撞之间划出了细微的区别。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 first-class</td><td>/ˌfɜːrstˈklæs/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>一流的，最好的</td><td>a first-class education<br>一流的教育</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The small college offers a first-class education at a modest price.  这所小学院以适中的价格提供一流的教育。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 first-rate</td><td>/ˌfɜːrst ˈreɪt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>一流的，极好的</td><td>a first-rate performance<br>一流的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The orchestra gave a first-rate performance of the new symphony.  乐团为这部新交响曲献上了一流的演出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 good</td><td>/ˈɡʊd/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有利的，好的</td><td>a good opportunity<br>有利的机会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This is a good moment to renegotiate the contract.  这是重新谈判合同的有利时机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 high-grade</td><td>/ˌhaɪ ˈɡreɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>高品质的，优质的</td><td>high-grade steel<br>优质钢材</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bridge was built from high-grade steel imported from Japan.  这座桥用从日本进口的优质钢材建造。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 superior</td><td>/suːˈpɪriər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>更好的，占优势的；上级</td><td>superior to the rest<br>优于其余各方</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This model is clearly superior to the older version.  这个型号明显优于旧版。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
